## IL-LABOUR-EPF-ESIC.md
---

# EPF & ESIC - Provident Fund & Health Insurance Framework

## Protocol ID
**IL-LABOUR-EPF-ESIC** (v1.0)

---

## Purpose
Guide employers and HR professionals through statutory compliance requirements under the Employees' Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act 1952 and the Employees' State Insurance Corporation Act 1948, covering applicability thresholds, contribution calculations, wage ceilings, EPF withdrawal procedures, ESIC medical and cash benefits, registration processes, monthly compliance (ECR/challan), UAN/IP number management, and penalties for non-compliance.

---

## Legal Framework

### 1. Dual Social Security System

**India's Social Security Coverage**:

| **Scheme** | **Governing Act** | **Coverage** | **Purpose** | **Contribution** |
|------------|-------------------|--------------|-------------|------------------|
| **EPF (Employees' Provident Fund)** | EPF Act 1952 | Retirement savings, pension | Long-term savings for post-retirement income | 12% employer + 12% employee |
| **ESIC (Employees' State Insurance)** | ESI Act 1948 | Health insurance, sickness/maternity benefits | Short-term cash + medical benefits during illness/injury | 3.25% employer + 0.75% employee |

**Overlap**: Many employees covered by BOTH EPF and ESIC (if establishment has ≥20 employees for EPF AND employee's wage ≤₹21,000/month for ESIC)

---

## EMPLOYEES' PROVIDENT FUND (EPF)

### 1. Applicability - Section 1(3)

**Mandatory Coverage**:
```
Section 1(3): This Act applies to every establishment which is a factory engaged
in any industry specified in Schedule I and in which twenty or more persons are
employed.

Once applicable, coverage continues EVEN IF employee count falls below 20.
```

**Coverage Criteria**:

| **Criteria** | **Threshold** | **Example** |
|--------------|---------------|-------------|
| **Employee Count** | ≥20 persons employed | Factory with 25 workers → EPF applies |
| **Industry Type** | Any industry in Schedule I (183 industries listed) | Manufacturing, construction, hotels, IT services (added 2010), cinemas, newspapers |
| **Wage Ceiling** | ₹15,000/month Basic + DA (for contribution calculation) | Employee earning ₹50,000 Basic → Contribution calculated ONLY on ₹15,000 |

**Schedule I Industries** (Major Categories):
1. Manufacturing (textiles, chemicals, pharmaceuticals, electronics)
2. Construction & civil engineering
3. Hotels, restaurants, eating houses
4. Cinemas, theatres, cultural entertainment
5. Newspapers, magazines, periodicals
6. Road motor transport
7. Wholesale/retail trade (if ≥50 employees)
8. IT services, software development (added via amendment)
9. Security services
10. Cleaning services

**Exemptions**:
- Establishments with <20 employees (unless voluntarily covered)
- Establishments granted exemption under Section 17 (if they have their own provident fund scheme with benefits ≥EPF)

### 2. Contribution Structure (Section 6)

**Total Contribution**: 24% of Basic + DA (12% employer + 12% employee)

**Bifurcation**:

**EMPLOYER'S 12% Contribution**:
```
A. EPF (Employees' Provident Fund): 3.67% of Basic + DA
   - Goes to employee's EPF account (retirement savings)

B. EPS (Employees' Pension Scheme): 8.33% of Basic + DA (capped at ₹15,000 Basic)
   - Goes to pension fund (managed by EPFO)
   - Provides monthly pension after age 58 (if ≥10 years membership)

C. EDLI (Employees' Deposit Linked Insurance): 0.5% of Basic + DA (max ₹15,000)
   - Life insurance cover (₹7 lakh max, payable to nominee upon employee's death)

Total Employer: 3.67% + 8.33% + 0.5% = 12.5% (employer pays 12% + 0.5% admin charges)
```

**EMPLOYEE'S 12% Contribution**:
```
100% goes to EPF account (employee's retirement savings)
```

**Calculation Example**:

```
EMPLOYEE PROFILE:
Name: Priya Sharma
Basic Salary: ₹30,000/month
Dearness Allowance (DA): ₹5,000/month
Total Basic + DA: ₹35,000/month

EPF WAGE CEILING: ₹15,000 (statutory ceiling)
Contribution calculated on: ₹15,000 (NOT ₹35,000)

MONTHLY CONTRIBUTIONS:

A. EMPLOYEE'S CONTRIBUTION:
   12% of ₹15,000 = ₹1,800/month
   Deducted from salary, goes to EPF account

B. EMPLOYER'S CONTRIBUTION:
   Total: 12% of ₹15,000 = ₹1,800/month

   Bifurcation:
   - EPF (3.67%): ₹551/month → Employee's EPF account
   - EPS (8.33%): ₹1,250/month → Pension fund
   - Total to EPF account: ₹1,800 (employee) + ₹551 (employer EPF) = ₹2,351/month

C. EDLI (0.5%): ₹75/month → Insurance fund
D. Admin charges (0.5%): ₹75/month → EPFO operational costs

TOTAL EMPLOYER OUTGO: ₹1,800 + ₹75 + ₹75 = ₹1,950/month

EMPLOYEE'S EPF BALANCE GROWTH (Annual):
Monthly addition: ₹2,351
Annual addition: ₹2,351 × 12 = ₹28,212
After 10 years (with 8.25% interest compounded annually): ~₹4.3 lakh
After 30 years: ~₹35 lakh (retirement corpus)
```

**Higher Contribution Option** (Voluntary):
- Employee can opt to contribute 12% on ACTUAL Basic (₹35,000) instead of capped ₹15,000
- Employer MUST match (if employee opts for higher contribution)
- Example: Employee contributes 12% of ₹35,000 = ₹4,200/month
           Employer contributes 12% of ₹35,000 = ₹4,200/month
           Total: ₹8,400/month (vs ₹2,351 under capped contribution)

### 3. EPF Withdrawal - Rules & Procedures

**Withdrawal Scenarios**:

**A. RETIREMENT (Age 58+)**:
```
Condition: Employee reaches age 58 OR retires (whichever earlier)
Withdrawal: 100% of EPF balance (employee's contribution + employer's EPF share
            + accumulated interest)
Tax: FULLY TAX-FREE (if service ≥5 years)
Process: Submit Form 19 (EPF withdrawal) + Form 10D (pension withdrawal)
Timeline: 15-30 days (if UAN, Aadhaar, bank account linked, online claim processed)
```

**B. UNEMPLOYMENT (>2 Months)**:
```
Condition: Resigned/terminated + unemployed for >2 months

Partial Withdrawal (After 1 Month Unemployment):
- Withdraw: Up to 75% of EPF balance
- Tax: Taxable (if service <5 years, employer's contribution taxed as salary)
- Process: Submit Form 19, unemployment declaration

Full Withdrawal (After 2 Months Unemployment):
- Withdraw: 100% of EPF balance
- Tax: Tax-free (if service ≥5 years), else employer's contribution taxable
- Process: Submit Form 19, unemployment certificate (from previous employer or
  self-declaration if <1 year service)
```

**C. TRANSFER TO NEW EMPLOYER**:
```
Condition: Joined new employer (also EPF-covered)
Action: Transfer EPF from previous employer to new employer (via UAN)
Process: AUTOMATIC (if both employers use same UAN, no withdrawal needed)
         New employer updates new employment details in UAN portal

Benefits of Transfer:
- Continuity of service (for pension eligibility, need ≥10 years continuous membership)
- No tax liability (funds remain in EPF, not withdrawn)
- Accumulated corpus continues earning interest
```

**D. ADVANCE FOR SPECIFIC PURPOSES** (Partial Withdrawal):

**Home Loan (Form 31)**:
```
Condition: Employee purchasing/constructing house (own name or joint with spouse)
Withdrawal: Lesser of:
            (a) 36 months Basic + DA, OR
            (b) Actual cost of house, OR
            (c) 90% of EPF balance

Example:
EPF Balance: ₹10 lakh
Basic + DA: ₹30,000/month
Home Purchase Cost: ₹50 lakh

Option (a): 36 × ₹30,000 = ₹10.8 lakh
Option (b): ₹50 lakh
Option (c): 90% of ₹10L = ₹9 lakh

Withdrawal Allowed: ₹9 lakh (LOWEST of three options)

Documents: Home loan sanction letter, sale deed/builder agreement, Form 31
```

**Medical Emergency (Form 31)**:
```
Condition: Hospitalization of self, spouse, children, or parents (serious illness)
Withdrawal: Up to 6 months Basic + DA OR cost of treatment, whichever LOWER

Example:
Medical bill: ₹5 lakh (cancer treatment)
Basic + DA: ₹30,000/month
6 months: ₹1.8 lakh

Withdrawal Allowed: ₹1.8 lakh (lower of ₹5L vs ₹1.8L)

Documents: Medical bills, hospitalization certificate, doctor's prescription
```

**Marriage/Education (Form 31)**:
```
Condition: Marriage of self, children, or siblings / Education of children
Withdrawal: Up to 50% of employee's contribution (NOT employer's share)
Eligibility: After 7 years membership

Example:
EPF Balance: ₹10 lakh (Employee ₹5L + Employer ₹5L)
Withdrawal: 50% of ₹5L = ₹2.5 lakh (only employee's share)

Documents: Marriage invitation, admission letter (college/school)
```

### 4. UAN (Universal Account Number)

**What is UAN**:
- 12-digit unique number (e.g., 101234567890) assigned to EACH employee
- Remains SAME throughout career (even when changing jobs, employer, city)
- Links all EPF accounts (from different employers) under one UAN

**Benefits**:
1. **Single Account**: All EPF contributions (from multiple employers) visible in one UAN portal
2. **Online Withdrawal**: Can withdraw EPF online (if UAN activated, Aadhaar linked, bank account seeded)
3. **Auto Transfer**: When changing jobs, EPF auto-transfers to new employer (no manual intervention)
4. **Transparency**: Check EPF balance, passbook, claim status anytime via UAN portal

**UAN Activation Process**:
```
STEP 1: Employer generates UAN (for new employee, via Unified Portal)

STEP 2: Employee activates UAN
- Visit: https://unifiedportal-mem.epfindia.gov.in/memberinterface/
- Click "Activate UAN"
- Enter: UAN, Mobile Number, Member ID (format: KN/12345/1234567), Aadhaar
- Set password

STEP 3: Link Aadhaar
- Login to UAN portal → Manage → Link Aadhaar
- Enter Aadhaar number → OTP sent to Aadhaar-registered mobile → Verify

STEP 4: Seed Bank Account
- Login → Manage → KYC → Bank
- Enter: Bank name, IFSC, Account number, upload cancelled cheque
- Bank account seeded (for direct EPF credit during withdrawal)

STEP 5: Ready for Online Claims
- Can now submit Form 19 (EPF withdrawal), Form 31 (advance) online
- No need to visit EPFO office, employer's signature not required
```

---

## EMPLOYEES' STATE INSURANCE (ESIC)

### 1. Applicability - Section 1(4)

**Mandatory Coverage**:
```
Section 1(4): This Act applies to factories or establishments wherein ten or more
persons are employed or were employed on any day of the preceding twelve months.

Note: Some states have higher threshold (≥20 employees in certain states)
```

**Coverage Criteria**:

| **Criteria** | **Threshold** | **Details** |
|--------------|---------------|-------------|
| **Employee Count** | ≥10 persons (some states ≥20) | Once applicable, continues even if count falls below threshold |
| **Wage Ceiling** | ≤₹21,000/month gross wages | Employees earning >₹21,000/month NOT covered |
| **Industry Type** | Non-seasonal factories/establishments | Manufacturing, shops, hotels, cinemas, medical institutions |

**Wage Ceiling Enforcement**:
```
EXAMPLE:
Employee A: Gross salary ₹18,000/month → COVERED (≤₹21,000)
Employee B: Gross salary ₹25,000/month → NOT COVERED (>₹21,000)

If Employee B's salary increases from ₹20,000 to ₹22,000:
- Coverage continues (once covered, remains covered even if wage exceeds ₹21,000)
- Contributions calculated on ₹21,000 ceiling (not ₹22,000)
```

### 2. Contribution Structure (Section 40)

**Total Contribution**: 4% of gross wages (3.25% employer + 0.75% employee)

**Calculation Example**:
```
EMPLOYEE PROFILE:
Name: Suresh Kumar
Gross Salary: ₹18,000/month
- Basic: ₹10,000
- HRA: ₹5,000
- Conveyance: ₹1,500
- Special Allowance: ₹1,500

ESIC WAGE: ₹18,000 (entire gross, unlike EPF which uses only Basic + DA)

MONTHLY CONTRIBUTIONS:

A. EMPLOYER'S CONTRIBUTION:
   3.25% of ₹18,000 = ₹585/month

B. EMPLOYEE'S CONTRIBUTION:
   0.75% of ₹18,000 = ₹135/month

TOTAL: ₹720/month (₹8,640/year)

EMPLOYEE'S TAKE-HOME:
Gross: ₹18,000
Less: ESIC (0.75%): ₹135
Less: EPF (12% of ₹15,000): ₹1,800
Less: Professional Tax: ₹200
NET: ₹18,000 - ₹135 - ₹1,800 - ₹200 = ₹15,865/month
```

### 3. ESIC Benefits

**A. MEDICAL BENEFIT** (Section 46):
```
Coverage: Free medical treatment for employee + family (spouse, children up to
          age 25, dependent parents)

Facilities:
- ESIC hospitals/dispensaries (OPD + IPD)
- Empaneled private hospitals (if ESIC hospital not available within 8 km radius)
- Medicines, diagnostics, surgeries (all free, no charges)

Procedure:
1. Employee gets ESIC IP (Insured Person) number upon enrollment
2. Family members added to IP card
3. Visit ESIC dispensary/hospital (show IP card)
4. Consultation, medicines, tests FREE

Limitations:
- Limited to ESIC network (cannot claim reimbursement for non-ESIC hospital treatment
  unless emergency + prior approval)
- Quality of ESIC hospitals variable (some well-equipped, others basic)
```

**B. SICKNESS BENEFIT** (Section 48):
```
Condition: Employee falls sick (non-occupational illness), unable to work
Benefit: 70% of average daily wages for up to 91 days in a year
Eligibility: Must have contributed for ≥78 days in preceding 6 months

Calculation Example:
Average Daily Wage: ₹18,000/26 = ₹692/day
Sickness Benefit: 70% of ₹692 = ₹484/day

Employee sick for 15 days (hospitalized for viral fever):
Total Benefit: ₹484 × 15 days = ₹7,260 (paid by ESIC, employer doesn't deduct leave)

Procedure:
1. Employee gets medical certificate from ESIC doctor (certifying sick leave)
2. Submits certificate to employer + ESIC
3. ESIC pays benefit directly to employee's bank account (within 30 days)
```

**C. MATERNITY BENEFIT** (Section 50):
```
Condition: Female employee pregnant, delivery, miscarriage
Benefit: 100% of average daily wages for 26 weeks (amended 2017, earlier 12 weeks)
Eligibility: Must have contributed for ≥70 days in preceding 2 contribution periods

Payment:
- 26 weeks = 182 days
- Daily wage: ₹18,000/26 = ₹692
- Total Maternity Benefit: ₹692 × 182 days = ₹1,25,944

Additional Benefits:
- Free prenatal care (ESIC hospitals provide checkups, sonography, delivery)
- Free postnatal care (6 weeks, mother + newborn)
- Medical bonus: ₹2,500 (if delivery at non-ESIC hospital, reimbursement)

Procedure:
1. Employee submits Form 102 (maternity benefit claim) to employer
2. Attach: Doctor's certificate (expected delivery date), birth certificate (post-delivery)
3. ESIC disburses benefit in 2 installments (first at 8 months pregnancy, second post-delivery)
```

**D. DISABLEMENT BENEFIT** (Section 52-54):

**Temporary Disablement**:
```
Condition: Work-related injury/accident, employee temporarily unable to work
Benefit: 90% of average daily wages for duration of disablement
Example: Factory worker injures hand (machine accident), 3 months recovery
         Daily wage: ₹692, Benefit: 90% × ₹692 = ₹623/day × 90 days = ₹56,070
```

**Permanent Disablement**:
```
Condition: Work-related injury causing permanent disability (loss of limb, blindness,
          permanent incapacity)
Benefit: 90% of average daily wages AS MONTHLY PENSION (for life)
Example: Construction worker falls from height, loses leg (permanent disability)
         Monthly pension: 90% × ₹18,000 = ₹16,200/month (lifelong)

Dependents' Benefit (if employee dies):
If employee dies due to work-related injury, dependents (spouse, children) receive
90% of wages as monthly pension (till spouse remarries, children reach age 25)
```

### 4. ESIC IP Number & Registration

**IP (Insured Person) Number**:
- 17-digit number (e.g., 1234567891234567-0) assigned to each employee
- Linked to employee's Aadhaar, family details
- Used for availing medical benefits, claiming cash benefits

**Registration Process**:
```
EMPLOYER'S ROLE:

STEP 1: Establishment Registration
- Register establishment on ESIC portal (within 15 days of applicability)
- Obtain 17-digit Employer Code

STEP 2: Employee Enrollment
- Add employee details (name, Aadhaar, date of joining, wages) on ESIC portal
- Generate IP number for each employee

STEP 3: IP Card Issuance
- ESIC issues physical IP card (to employee's address, 30-45 days)
- Temporary IP number usable immediately for medical benefits

EMPLOYEE'S ROLE:

STEP 4: Family Declaration
- Employee submits Form 1A (declaration of family members)
- Attach: Spouse's Aadhaar, children's birth certificates, parents' age proof

STEP 5: Aadhaar Seeding
- Link Aadhaar to IP number (via ESIC portal or at ESIC branch)
- Enables direct benefit transfer (DBT) to bank account
```

---

## Compliance Requirements

### 1. Monthly Compliance - ECR (Electronic Challan cum Return)

**EPF Compliance**:

```
MONTHLY ECR FILING (by 15th of next month):

STEP 1: Prepare wage data
- Employee-wise Basic + DA, EPF/EPS contribution
- Joiners, leavers, UAN details

STEP 2: Upload ECR on Unified Portal
- Login: https://unifiedportal-emp.epfindia.gov.in/epfo/
- Upload: ECR file (Excel format with employee details, contributions)

STEP 3: Generate Challan
- System auto-generates challan (total EPF + EPS + EDLI + admin charges)

STEP 4: Payment
- Pay via NEFT/RTGS to EPFO account (by 15th)
- Payment reference number captured in portal

STEP 5: Verification
- EPFO verifies payment within 2-3 days
- EPF credits to individual employees' accounts (visible in passbook within 7 days)

PENALTY FOR DELAY:
- 5% p.a. damages (Section 7Q) if payment delayed beyond 15th
- 12% p.a. damages if delay >60 days
- Criminal prosecution under Section 14 (imprisonment up to 3 years + fine)
```

**ESIC Compliance**:

```
MONTHLY ESIC RETURN (by 11th of next month):

STEP 1: Generate Monthly Contribution
- Employee-wise gross wages, ESIC contribution (3.25% + 0.75%)

STEP 2: File Return on ESIC Portal
- Login: https://www.esic.in/
- Upload: Monthly contribution details

STEP 3: Generate Challan & Pay
- Pay via online banking (by 15th of next month)

STEP 4: Reconciliation
- ESIC matches payment with return, updates employee accounts

PENALTY FOR DELAY:
- 12% p.a. damages on delayed contribution
- Criminal prosecution (imprisonment up to 2 years + fine ₹10,000 per Section 85)
```

### 2. Annual Compliance

**EPF Annual Return**:
```
Form 3A (Annual Return): Due by 30th April each year
- Total employees covered during year (average, max, min headcount)
- Total contributions paid (employer + employee)
- Any exemptions claimed, disputes pending

Form 6A (Annual Statement): Due by 30th April
- Employee-wise contribution statement for entire year
- Verifies monthly ECRs aggregated correctly
```

**ESIC Annual Return**:
```
Form 5 (Annual Return): Due by 30th April each year
- Total employees covered, total wages paid
- Total contributions paid (employer + employee)
- Medical benefits availed (number of employees treated, cash benefits paid)
```

---

## Penalties & Prosecution

### 1. EPF Penalties (Section 14)

**Non-Remittance of Contributions**:
```
Section 14: Punishment for non-payment of contributions
If employer:
(a) Deducts EPF from employee's salary BUT doesn't deposit with EPFO, OR
(b) Fails to pay employer's contribution

Penalty:
- Imprisonment: 1-3 years (extendable to 5 years if repeat offender)
- Fine: No limit (at court's discretion, typically ₹10-50 lakh)
- Damages: 12% p.a. on delayed amount (Section 7Q)

Example Case:
ABC Pvt Ltd (100 employees) deducted ₹5 lakh EPF from salaries (12 months)
BUT deposited only ₹2 lakh (₹3 lakh misappropriated)

Criminal Case:
- Employer's MD arrested, charged under Section 14
- Fine: ₹25 lakh + Imprisonment: 2 years (sentenced)
- Civil Recovery: ₹3 lakh principal + 12% p.a. damages (₹36,000) = ₹3.36 lakh
- Total Liability: ₹28.36 lakh + 2 years jail term
```

### 2. ESIC Penalties (Section 85)

**Non-Remittance of Contributions**:
```
Section 85: Penalty for non-payment of contribution
If employer fails to pay ESIC contributions:

Penalty:
- Imprisonment: Up to 2 years
- Fine: Up to ₹10,000
- Damages: 12% p.a. on delayed contribution

Recovery: ESIC can recover as land revenue (attachment of property, bank accounts)
```

---

## Practical Examples

### Example 1: Startup Compliance (First-Time EPF/ESIC)

```
COMPANY: XYZ Tech Solutions Pvt Ltd (IT startup, Bengaluru)
EMPLOYEE COUNT: 25 employees (crossed 20-employee threshold in Month 6)

EPF APPLICABILITY:
Month 6: 25 employees (≥20 threshold) → EPF applicable from Day 1 of Month 7

ESIC APPLICABILITY:
Karnataka threshold: ≥10 employees → ESIC applicable from Day 1 of Month 4
(when employee count reached 10)

COMPLIANCE ACTIONS:

MONTH 7 (EPF Kicks In):
1. Register establishment on EPF Unified Portal (within 30 days)
2. Obtain EPFO Establishment Code (KN/12345/000001)
3. Generate UAN for all 25 employees
4. File first ECR (Month 7 contributions by 15th of Month 8)

MONTH 4 (ESIC Kicks In):
1. Register establishment on ESIC portal (within 15 days)
2. Obtain ESIC Employer Code (12-34-567890-000-0000)
3. Enroll all employees (wage ≤₹21,000) → 15 employees eligible (10 earn >₹21K)
4. Generate IP numbers for 15 employees
5. File first ESIC return (Month 4 contributions by 15th of Month 5)

MONTHLY PAYROLL (Ongoing):
Month 7 onwards: Deduct EPF (12%) + ESIC (0.75% if applicable) from all employees
Month 8, 15th: File ECR, pay EPF contributions (employer + employee)
Month 8, 15th: File ESIC return, pay ESIC contributions

EMPLOYEE COMMUNICATION:
- Share UAN numbers via email (employees activate UAN, link Aadhaar)
- Share IP numbers (employees collect IP cards from ESIC branch)
- Conduct session on EPF withdrawal, ESIC medical benefits (HR orientation)
```

### Example 2: EPF Withdrawal - Unemployment Scenario

```
EMPLOYEE: Priya Sharma (resigned from XYZ Tech, unemployed)
EPF BALANCE: ₹5,00,000 (accumulated over 4 years)
SERVICE: 4 years (less than 5 years, so withdrawal taxable)

WITHDRAWAL OPTIONS:

OPTION A: Full Withdrawal (After 2 Months Unemployment)
- Wait 2 months post-resignation
- Submit Form 19 online (via UAN portal)
- Withdrawal: ₹5,00,000 (100%)
- Tax: Employer's share (₹2,50,000) taxable as salary (added to FY income)
       Assuming 30% tax bracket: Tax = ₹75,000
- Net Receipt: ₹5,00,000 (tax deducted during FY tax filing, not at withdrawal)
- Timeline: 7-10 days (direct credit to bank account)

OPTION B: Partial Withdrawal (After 1 Month)
- Submit Form 19 after 1 month unemployment
- Withdrawal: 75% of ₹5L = ₹3,75,000
- Tax: Proportionate (₹1,87,500 employer's share taxable)
- Remaining Balance: ₹1,25,000 continues in EPF (if rejoins EPF-covered job,
  gets transferred automatically via UAN)

OPTION C: Transfer to New Employer (No Withdrawal)
- Joined new employer (also EPF-covered) in Month 3
- No withdrawal, UAN auto-transfers EPF to new employer
- Benefits: Service continuity (if completes 5 years total, withdrawal tax-free),
           compound interest continues on ₹5L corpus
- BEST OPTION if new employment within 2 months (avoid tax, maximize retirement corpus)
```

---

## Compliance Checklist

### For Employers (EPF & ESIC)

**EPF Compliance**:
- [ ] Register establishment on Unified Portal (within 30 days of crossing 20 employees)
- [ ] Generate UAN for all employees (within 1 month of joining)
- [ ] File ECR by 15th of every month (wage data + contributions)
- [ ] Pay EPF contributions by 15th (employer + employee share)
- [ ] File Form 3A, 6A by 30th April each year (annual returns)
- [ ] Respond to EPFO notices within 30 days (clarifications, audits)
- [ ] Maintain EPF register (employee-wise contribution records, 3 years)

**ESIC Compliance**:
- [ ] Register establishment on ESIC portal (within 15 days of crossing 10 employees threshold)
- [ ] Enroll eligible employees (wage ≤₹21,000/month) within 10 days of joining
- [ ] Generate IP numbers, issue IP cards (coordinate with ESIC branch)
- [ ] File monthly return by 11th of next month (contribution details)
- [ ] Pay ESIC contributions by 15th of next month
- [ ] File Form 5 by 30th April each year (annual return)
- [ ] Display ESIC notice at workplace (benefits, nearest dispensary details)

### For Employees (EPF & ESIC)

**EPF**:
- [ ] Activate UAN within 1 month of joining (set password, link Aadhaar, seed bank account)
- [ ] Check EPF passbook quarterly (verify contributions credited)
- [ ] Transfer EPF via UAN when changing jobs (no withdrawal needed if new employer EPF-covered)
- [ ] Withdraw EPF after retirement OR unemployment >2 months (Form 19 online)
- [ ] Claim advances for home loan, medical emergency (Form 31 online)
- [ ] Update KYC (Aadhaar, PAN, bank account) to avoid claim rejections

**ESIC**:
- [ ] Collect IP card from ESIC branch (within 2 months of enrollment)
- [ ] Add family members to IP card (Form 1A, submit within 3 months)
- [ ] Avail medical benefits (visit ESIC dispensary, show IP card, free treatment)
- [ ] Claim sickness benefit if hospitalized (submit medical certificate from ESIC doctor)
- [ ] Female employees: Claim maternity benefit (Form 102, 8 months into pregnancy)

## Landmark Cases

### 1. Regional Provident Fund Commissioner v. Shiv Kumar Joshi (SC 2005)

**Facts**:
- Employer deducted ₹10 lakh EPF from 100 employees over 12 months
- Deposited only ₹4 lakh with EPFO (₹6 lakh misappropriated)
- EPFO filed criminal case under Section 14 (non-remittance of contributions)

**Employer's Defense**:
- Company in financial crisis (losses for 3 years)
- Used EPF money to pay salaries (prevented closure, saved 100 jobs)
- Pleaded for leniency (no criminal intent, only business compulsion)

**Supreme Court Held**:
1. **EPF is Trust Money**: Employee's PF contribution is held in TRUST by employer (not employer's money)
2. **Misappropriation = Criminal Breach of Trust**: Using EPF for business purposes (even to pay salaries) is criminal offense (Section 406 IPC + Section 14 EPF Act)
3. **No Defense of Financial Crisis**: Financial hardship NOT a valid defense for EPF misappropriation
4. **Conviction Upheld**: Employer's MD sentenced to 2 years imprisonment + ₹10 lakh fine

**Principle**: EPF contributions are sacrosanct, employer CANNOT use for any purpose other than depositing with EPFO

## IL-LABOUR-ESI-COMPLIANCE.md
---

# IL-LABOUR-ESI-COMPLIANCE

## Protocol ID
IL-LABOUR-ESI-COMPLIANCE

## Classification
- **Domain**: Labour Law
- **Sub-domain**: Statutory Compliance
- **Type**: Operational Protocol
- **Complexity**: High
- **Version**: 1.0.0

---

## Purpose
This protocol provides comprehensive guidance on compliance with the Employees' State Insurance Act 1948, covering registration, contribution calculations, medical benefits, cash benefits (sickness, maternity, disability, dependents), claims procedures, inspections, and penalties.

---

## Core Framework

### 1. APPLICABILITY (Section 1, 2)

#### 1.1 Coverage
```
ESI (EMPLOYEES' STATE INSURANCE) ACT 1948:

MANDATORY FOR:
1. Factories using power:
   - ≥10 employees (any day in preceding 12 months)

2. Factories not using power:
   - ≥20 employees (any day)

3. Shops, establishments, restaurants, hotels, cinemas, road transport:
   - As notified by State Government (usually ≥10 employees)

4. Educational, medical institutions (private):
   - As notified (varies by state)

WAGE LIMIT (Section 2(9)):
Applies to employees earning ≤₹21,000/month

CALCULATION:
Gross wages (Basic + DA + HRA + All allowances + Bonus)
If Gross >₹21,000/month → NOT covered under ESI
If Gross ≤₹21,000/month → MANDATORY coverage

EXAMPLE:
Employee A: Basic ₹15,000 + DA ₹3,000 + HRA ₹2,500 = ₹20,500 (ESI applicable)
Employee B: Basic ₹18,000 + DA ₹5,000 + HRA ₹3,000 = ₹26,000 (ESI NOT applicable)

EMPLOYEE DEFINITION:
Person employed for wages (skilled, unskilled, manual, clerical, supervisory)
Excludes: Managerial, administrative staff (if wage >₹21,000)

ONCE COVERED, ALWAYS COVERED (till separation):
If employee's wage increases beyond ₹21,000 after joining:
→ ESI coverage continues (cannot drop mid-employment)
Exception: If wage >₹21,000 at joining → Never covered
```

---

### 2. REGISTRATION (Section 2A, 40)

#### 2.1 Employer Registration
```
MANDATORY (Section 2A):
Every employer (factory/establishment) covered by ESI Act must register

AUTHORITY:
ESIC (Employees' State Insurance Corporation) - Regional Office

PROCEDURE:

STEP 1: APPLICATION (online via ESIC portal www.esic.in)
Details:
- Employer name, address, type of establishment
- PAN, GST, Shops & Establishments registration (if applicable)
- Nature of business/manufacturing
- Number of employees (total, ESI-covered)
- Date of coverage (when ≥10/20 employees first employed)

STEP 2: DOCUMENTS TO UPLOAD
- PAN card (establishment)
- Cancelled cheque (employer's bank account for contribution deposits)
- Shops & Establishments certificate OR Factories Act registration
- List of employees (ESI-eligible, with Aadhaar, bank details)

STEP 3: CODE NUMBER ALLOTTED
ESIC allots 17-digit Code Number (unique employer ID)
Format: [State Code]-[Regional Office]-[Sub-regional]-[Branch]-[Employer Serial]

STEP 4: REGISTRATION CERTIFICATE
Downloadable from portal (PDF)
Display at establishment (prominently, accessible to employees)

TIMELINE:
Register within 15 days of Act becoming applicable

PENALTY FOR NON-REGISTRATION (Section 85):
Imprisonment up to 3 years + Fine up to ₹10,000
(Serious offense, strictly enforced)
```

#### 2.2 Employee Registration & Insurance Number
```
IP NUMBER (Insured Person Number):
Unique 17-digit number for each ESI-covered employee
Format: [Employer Code]-[Employee Serial Number]

ISSUANCE:
Employer registers employee on ESIC portal
Within 10 days of joining (if ESI-applicable)
Upload: Name, Father's name, DOB, Aadhaar, Bank account, Family details

FAMILY DETAILS (for medical benefits):
- Spouse
- Children (up to 2, dependent)
- Parents (if dependent, living with employee)

E-PEHCHAN CARD:
Issued to employee + family members
Biometric card (photo, fingerprint)
Used at ESIC hospitals/dispensaries for treatment

TEMPORARY IP NUMBER:
For new employees (till permanent IP generated)
Employer issues (on letterhead), valid for 3 months
Employee can avail treatment using temporary IP

PORTABILITY:
IP number is portable (if employee changes jobs, same IP number continues)
New employer links existing IP to their Code Number
```

---

### 3. CONTRIBUTIONS (Section 38, 39, 44)

#### 3.1 Contribution Rates
```
EMPLOYEE CONTRIBUTION: 0.75% of wages
EMPLOYER CONTRIBUTION: 3.25% of wages
TOTAL: 4% of wages

WAGES DEFINITION (for contribution calculation):
All remuneration paid/payable in cash (Basic + DA + HRA + Allowances + Overtime + Bonus)
Excludes: Reimbursements (travel, medical bills, conveyance if actual bills submitted)

CALCULATION EXAMPLE:
Employee wages: ₹18,000/month (Basic ₹12,000 + DA ₹3,000 + HRA ₹3,000)

Employee contribution: ₹18,000 × 0.75% = ₹135
Employer contribution: ₹18,000 × 3.25% = ₹585
Total contribution: ₹720/month

Annual: ₹720 × 12 = ₹8,640
```

#### 3.2 Contribution Payment
```
CHALLAN GENERATION:
Online via ESIC portal (monthly)
Due date: 15th of following month

PAYMENT MODE:
- Net banking (NEFT, RTGS from employer's bank account)
- Challan reference number generated after payment

TIMELINE:
Contribution for January 2025 → Pay by 15th February 2025

DELAYED PAYMENT:
- Interest: 12% p.a. (from due date till payment)
- Damages: Up to 25% of contribution amount (if delay >2 months)

EXAMPLE (Delayed Payment):
Total contribution due (Jan 2025): ₹50,000
Due date: 15th Feb 2025
Actual payment: 15th April 2025 (2 months delay)

Interest: ₹50,000 × 12% × 2/12 = ₹1,000
Damages: 25% × ₹50,000 = ₹12,500 (at ESIC's discretion)
Total: ₹50,000 + ₹1,000 + ₹12,500 = ₹63,500

CRIMINAL LIABILITY (Section 85):
Willful default (>6 months): Imprisonment up to 3 years + Fine
```

---

### 4. MEDICAL BENEFITS (Section 46)

#### 4.1 Coverage
```
BENEFICIARIES:
- Insured employee (IP holder)
- Spouse
- Children (up to 2, dependent, unmarried)
- Parents (if dependent on IP, living with IP)

TREATMENT:
FREE OF COST (no payment at ESIC facility)
- Outpatient care (OPD): Dispensaries, polyclinics
- Inpatient care (IPD): ESIC hospitals
- Specialist consultations
- Diagnostic tests (X-ray, blood tests, ultrasound, CT/MRI if medically necessary)
- Medicines (free from ESIC pharmacy)
- Surgery, hospitalization (all costs borne by ESIC)

FACILITIES:
1. ESIC Dispensaries (primary care):
   - General physician consultation
   - Basic medicines
   - Referral to hospital (if specialist needed)

2. ESIC Hospitals (secondary/tertiary care):
   - Multi-specialty (cardiology, orthopedics, gynecology, pediatrics)
   - 24/7 emergency
   - Surgery (general, specialized)
   - ICU, NICU facilities

3. Tie-up Hospitals (if ESIC facility not available):
   - Private hospitals empaneled by ESIC
   - Cashless treatment (ESIC pays hospital directly)
   - Emergency cases (if nearest ESIC hospital >8 km)

DENTAL, OPHTHALMIC CARE:
Covered (spectacles, dental treatment, dentures - as per ESIC norms)

CHRONIC DISEASES:
Diabetes, hypertension, cancer, kidney disease → Lifelong treatment free

MATERNITY:
Pre-natal, delivery, post-natal care (covered under medical benefit + cash maternity benefit)
```

---

### 5. CASH BENEFITS

#### 5.1 Sickness Benefit (Section 46)
```
ELIGIBILITY: Employee contributed for ≥78 days in preceding 6 months

RATE: 70% of average daily wages
DURATION: Up to 91 days per year (extendable to 124 days if chronic disease)

CALCULATION:
Average daily wage = Total wages in preceding 4 months ÷ days for which wages paid

EXAMPLE:
Employee sick for 15 days (certified by ESIC doctor)
Last 4 months wages: ₹72,000 (₹18,000 × 4)
Days worked in 4 months: 104 days (26 days/month × 4)
Average daily wage: ₹72,000 ÷ 104 = ₹692/day
Sickness benefit: 70% × ₹692 × 15 days = ₹7,266

CLAIM PROCEDURE:
Submit medical certificate (ESIC doctor) + claim form within 14 days of illness
Benefit paid within 30 days (directly to employee's bank account)
```

#### 5.2 Maternity Benefit (Section 50)
```
ELIGIBILITY: Woman employee contributed for ≥70 days in preceding 12 months

RATE: 100% of average daily wages (not 70%, full wages)
DURATION: 26 weeks (182 days) for first 2 children, 12 weeks for 3rd child onwards

CALCULATION:
Same average daily wage formula (total wages in preceding 4 months ÷ days worked)

EXAMPLE:
Maternity leave: 182 days
Last 4 months wages: ₹80,000
Days worked: 104 days
Average daily wage: ₹80,000 ÷ 104 = ₹769/day
Maternity benefit: 100% × ₹769 × 182 days = ₹1,39,958

MEDICAL BONUS: ₹3,500 (if no pre/post-natal care at ESIC facility)

CLAIM: Submit expected delivery date certificate, actual delivery certificate
Payment: 50% advance (6 weeks before delivery), 50% after delivery
```

---

## Compliance Checklist

### For Employers
- [ ] Register establishment within 15 days (if ≥10/20 employees, wages ≤₹21,000)
- [ ] Register employees within 10 days of joining (upload to ESIC portal)
- [ ] Deduct employee contribution 0.75%, add employer contribution 3.25%
- [ ] Pay contribution by 15th of following month (via ESIC portal, net banking)
- [ ] Issue E-Pehchan cards to employees + families (download from portal)
- [ ] Display ESIC registration certificate, contribution rates at establishment
- [ ] Maintain records (contribution challans, employee master list for 5 years)

### For Employees
- [ ] Verify IP number issued (within 1 month of joining)
- [ ] Download E-Pehchan card (carry to ESIC facility for treatment)
- [ ] Register family members (spouse, children, parents) for medical coverage
- [ ] Avail treatment at ESIC dispensary/hospital (free, cashless)
- [ ] File sickness benefit claim within 14 days (with ESIC doctor certificate)
- [ ] File maternity benefit claim in advance (6 weeks before expected delivery)

---

## Penalties (Section 85)
- Non-registration: Imprisonment up to 3 years + Fine ₹10,000
- Delayed contribution (>6 months): Imprisonment up to 3 years + Interest 12% + Damages 25%
- Not issuing IP number: Fine ₹5,000-₹25,000
- False claims: Imprisonment up to 1 year + Fine + Refund of fraudulent benefit

---

## Conclusion

ESI provides comprehensive medical and cash benefits to low-wage workers (≤₹21,000/month), covering 13 crore workers across India. Key compliance: Register employer + employees, contribute 4% (0.75% employee + 3.25% employer) by 15th monthly, ensure employees avail free treatment at ESIC facilities. Benefits include lifelong medical care (family included), sickness benefit (70% wages × 91 days), maternity (100% wages × 182 days), disability pension, and dependents benefit. Low contribution cost (₹720/month for ₹18K salary) delivers significant value (lakhs in medical coverage annually). Strict enforcement (imprisonment for non-registration, heavy interest/damages for delays) ensures compliance.

---

## Version History
| Version | Date | Changes | Author |
|---------|------|---------|--------|
| 1.0.0 | 2025-12-06 | Initial protocol creation | Claude Brain |

---

## References
- Employees' State Insurance Act 1948 (Central Act 34 of 1948)
- ESI (Central) Rules 1950
- ESIC Portal: www.esic.in

---

**END OF PROTOCOL**


---
## IL-LABOUR-FACTORIES-ACT.md
---

# IL-LABOUR-FACTORIES-ACT

## Protocol ID
IL-LABOUR-FACTORIES-ACT

## Classification
- **Domain**: Labour Law
- **Sub-domain**: Statutory Compliance
- **Type**: Operational Protocol
- **Complexity**: High
- **Version**: 1.0.0

---

## Purpose
This protocol provides comprehensive guidance on compliance with the Factories Act 1948, covering registration, licensing, health, safety, welfare provisions, working hours, leave entitlements, employment of women and children, inspections, and penalties for violations.

---

## Core Framework

### 1. APPLICABILITY & DEFINITIONS (Section 2)

#### 1.1 What is a Factory
```
FACTORY (Section 2(m)):
Any premises where:
- 10 OR MORE workers are employed (if using power), OR
- 20 OR MORE workers are employed (without power)
AND
- Manufacturing process is carried out

MANUFACTURING PROCESS (Section 2(k)):
Includes:
✓ Making, altering, repairing, ornamenting, finishing any article or substance
✓ Assembling, packing, oiling, washing, cleaning, breaking up, demolishing
✓ Pumping oil, water, sewage
✓ Generating, transforming, transmitting electricity or other power
✓ Printing, bookbinding, lithography

EXCLUDES:
✗ Mines (covered by Mines Act 1952)
✗ Mobile units (belonging to armed forces)
✗ Hotels, restaurants, shops (covered by S&E Acts)
✗ Construction sites (covered by Building & Other Construction Workers Act)
```

#### 1.2 Threshold Calculation
```
COUNTING WORKERS:

EXAMPLE 1 - Power Factory:
ABC Manufacturing uses electric motors (power)
Workers employed:
- Permanent: 8
- Contract: 3 (through contractor, but working in factory)
- Part-time: 1 (counts as 1, not 0.5)
TOTAL: 12 workers

Since >10 workers + power used → FACTORY (Factories Act applies)

EXAMPLE 2 - Non-Power Factory:
XYZ Handicrafts (manual labor, no power)
Workers: 22 (all manual workers)
TOTAL: 22 workers

Since >20 workers (even without power) → FACTORY (Factories Act applies)

EXAMPLE 3 - Below Threshold:
Small Workshop: 9 workers, uses power
NOT a factory (need ≥10 for power factory)
Covered by: Shops & Establishments Act (state-specific)
```

---

### 2. REGISTRATION & LICENSING (Section 6, 7)

#### 2.1 Registration (Section 7)
```
MANDATORY:
Every factory must be registered with Chief Inspector of Factories (State)

PROCEDURE:

STEP 1: APPLICATION (Form 1)
Submit to Chief Inspector at least 60 days before commencement of production
Details:
- Name and address of factory
- Name of occupier (owner/manager)
- Name of factory manager
- Nature of manufacturing process
- Number of workers (estimated)
- Power installed (HP/KW)
- Working hours, shifts

STEP 2: INSPECTION
Inspector may visit factory (verify infrastructure, safety measures)

STEP 3: REGISTRATION CERTIFICATE
If satisfied: Certificate of Registration issued (permanent, unless revoked)

TIMELINE:
60 days notice required before starting operations

AMENDMENTS:
Any change (increase in workers, new process, change of occupier) → Notify Inspector (Form 3)
```

#### 2.2 License (Section 6)
```
ADDITIONAL REQUIREMENT (for certain processes):
Factories engaged in hazardous processes require LICENSE (beyond registration)

HAZARDOUS PROCESSES (Schedule I):
- Manufacture of explosives, fireworks
- Petroleum refining, LPG handling
- Chemical manufacturing (acids, alkalis, pesticides)
- Asbestos processing
- Radiation-related work
- Any process notified by State Government as hazardous

PROCEDURE:

STEP 1: APPLICATION (Form 7)
Submit to Chief Inspector
Attachments:
- Layout plan (factory premises, machinery locations)
- Safety plan (emergency exits, fire equipment, first aid)
- Environmental clearances (pollution control board)
- Proof of trained personnel (safety officers, first-aiders)

STEP 2: RIGOROUS INSPECTION
Inspector examines:
- Safety equipment (fire extinguishers, sprinklers, alarms)
- Ventilation systems (for toxic fumes)
- Emergency exits, escape routes
- Personal protective equipment (PPE) for workers
- Trained staff (safety officer mandatory)

STEP 3: LICENSE ISSUANCE
Valid for: 1 year (renewable annually)
Conditions: Compliance with safety norms, periodic inspections

RENEWAL:
Apply 30 days before expiry (Form 7A)
Inspector verifies continued compliance
Renewal for 1 year

REVOCATION:
If serious violations: License suspended/cancelled
→ Factory must stop operations (hazardous process)
```

---

### 3. HEALTH PROVISIONS (Chapter III, Section 11-20)

#### 3.1 Cleanliness (Section 11)
```
MANDATORY MEASURES:

1. DAILY CLEANING:
   - Floors cleaned daily (swept, mopped)
   - Machinery cleaned (oil, grease residue removed)
   - Walls, ceilings whitewashed/painted annually

2. WASTE DISPOSAL:
   - Separate bins for dry, wet, hazardous waste
   - Daily disposal (no accumulation)
   - Drainage system (wastewater, sewage)

3. SPITTOONS:
   - Provided at convenient places (if workers chew tobacco)
   - Cleaned daily

PENALTY (Section 92):
Non-compliance: Fine up to ₹1,00,000 (occupier)
```

#### 3.2 Ventilation & Temperature (Section 13, 14)
```
VENTILATION (Section 13):
- Adequate fresh air circulation (natural or mechanical)
- Enclosed workrooms: Fans, exhaust systems mandatory
- Toxic fumes/dust: Separate exhaust ducts (not to mix with general air)

TEMPERATURE (Section 14):
- Reasonable temperature (not oppressively hot/cold)
- Hot processes: Fans, air coolers provided
- No specific temperature range (unlike OSHA in US), but "comfortable for workers"
- Thermometers installed (to monitor)

LIGHTING (Section 17):
- Sufficient natural or artificial lighting
- Minimum: 200 lux (factory floor), 500 lux (precision work)
- Shadows, glare minimized (lamp shades, diffusers)
```

#### 3.3 Dust & Fume Control (Section 14)
```
DUSTY PROCESSES (textile, cement, flour mills):
- Dust extractors at source (machines)
- Workers provided masks (N95 or equivalent)
- Periodic medical checkup (lung function tests for workers)

TOXIC FUMES (chemical factories, welding):
- Exhaust hoods above workstations
- Ventilation ducts (fumes expelled outside factory, above roof level)
- Gas detectors (for toxic gas leaks: CO, ammonia, chlorine)
- Emergency alarms, evacuation plan

PERIODIC TESTING:
Air quality testing (annually by certified agency)
Dust concentration, toxic gas levels measured
Report submitted to Inspector
```

#### 3.4 Overcrowding (Section 16)
```
MAXIMUM DENSITY:
- Each worker must have at least 14.2 cubic meters (500 cubic feet) space
- Height of room included in calculation

CALCULATION EXAMPLE:
Factory floor area: 1,000 sq meters
Ceiling height: 5 meters
Cubic space: 1,000 × 5 = 5,000 cubic meters

Maximum workers allowed: 5,000 ÷ 14.2 = 352 workers

If 400 workers → OVERCROWDING (violation)

REMEDY:
- Reduce workforce to 352, OR
- Expand factory premises, OR
- Implement shift system (only 176 workers per shift, if 2 shifts)
```

---

### 4. SAFETY PROVISIONS (Chapter IV, Section 21-41)

#### 4.1 Fencing of Machinery (Section 21)
```
MANDATORY FENCING:
All moving parts of machinery must be securely fenced:
- Flywheels
- Gears, pulleys, belts
- Rotating shafts
- Cutting blades, grinding wheels

FENCING REQUIREMENTS:
- Strong construction (metal grill, polycarbonate sheets)
- Height: Sufficient to prevent accidental contact
- Access panels: Only trained workers can open (lockout/tagout system)

EXCEPTIONS:
- Prime movers (engines) in separate rooms (room itself is fence)
- Machinery inaccessible (height >2.5 meters, no ladder/platform)

PENALTY (Section 92):
Non-compliance: Imprisonment up to 2 years + Fine (if accident occurs due to non-fencing)
```

#### 4.2 Safe Operating Procedures (Section 23-28)
```
EMPLOYMENT ON DANGEROUS MACHINES (Section 23):
- Only trained workers (minimum 6 months training or certified)
- Certification by factory manager (worker competent to operate)

STRIKING GEAR & DEVICES (Section 24):
- Emergency stop buttons at each machine (easily accessible, red color)
- Functional testing (monthly, documented)

CASING OF NEW MACHINERY (Section 25):
- All new machinery must have built-in safety guards (manufacturer's responsibility)
- Factory accepts only if safety-compliant

PROHIBITED WORK (Section 26):
- Women, young persons (14-18 years) NOT allowed to clean, oil moving machinery
- Cleaning only when machine is stopped and locked out

PRESSURE VESSELS (Section 31):
- Boilers, air receivers (compressed air tanks) require:
  - Annual inspection by competent person (boiler inspector, certified engineer)
  - Pressure relief valves (automatic, tested quarterly)
  - Logbook (pressure readings, maintenance)

LIFTING MACHINERY (Section 28):
- Cranes, hoists, lifts:
  - Load capacity clearly marked (e.g., "Max Load 5 Tonnes")
  - Overloading prohibited
  - Annual inspection (certificate of fitness)
  - Operator trained and certified
```

#### 4.3 Fire Safety (Section 38)
```
FIRE PREVENTION:

1. FIRE EXITS:
   - Minimum 2 exits per floor (opposite sides)
   - Width: ≥1.5 meters (for evacuation)
   - Unobstructed, unlocked during working hours
   - Glow-in-dark signage ("EXIT")

2. FIRE EXTINGUISHERS:
   - Type A (water, for wood/paper fires): 1 per 100 sq meters
   - Type BC (CO2, for electrical/oil fires): 1 per electrical panel
   - Annual maintenance contract (AMC) with certified agency
   - Monthly inspection (pressure check, pin intact)

3. FIRE ALARM:
   - Manual call points (break glass alarms) at exits
   - Automatic smoke detectors (in storage areas, electrical rooms)
   - Audible alarm (loud enough to hear over machinery noise, >90 dB)

4. FIRE DRILL:
   - Conducted twice per year (simulate fire evacuation)
   - All workers participate
   - Timed (evacuation must complete in <5 minutes)
   - Documented (date, time, observations, lessons learned)

5. FIRE FIGHTING EQUIPMENT (large factories, >500 workers):
   - Fire hydrant system (water pipes, hoses)
   - Sprinkler system (automatic, heat-activated)
   - Fire brigade contract (if municipal fire station >5 km away)

NO-SMOKING POLICY:
- Prohibited in factory premises (except designated areas, away from flammable materials)
- Signage, enforcement by security
```

---

### 5. WELFARE PROVISIONS (Chapter V, Section 42-50)

#### 5.1 Drinking Water (Section 42)
```
REQUIREMENTS:
- Wholesome drinking water at convenient places
- Marked "DRINKING WATER" (in local language + English)
- Within 6 meters of every worker (no long walks)
- Coolers in summer (if ambient temperature >35°C)
- Water quality testing: Annually by certified lab

QUANTITY:
Minimum 5 liters per worker per day (for 8-hour shift)

EXAMPLE:
Factory with 200 workers, 2 shifts:
Water required: 200 × 2 shifts × 5 liters = 2,000 liters/day
Install: 4 water coolers (500 liter capacity each) + RO purifiers
```

#### 5.2 Latrines & Urinals (Section 42)
```
MINIMUM REQUIREMENT:

TOILETS:
- 1 toilet per 25 workers (separate for men and women)
- Sanitary, clean (cleaned at least twice per shift)
- Adequate lighting, water supply
- Flush toilets (not dry latrines in urban areas)

URINALS (for men):
- 1 urinal per 50 male workers
- Partition walls (privacy)
- Constant water flow (flushing)

EXAMPLE:
Factory with 100 male workers, 50 female workers

Men:
- Toilets: 100 ÷ 25 = 4 toilets
- Urinals: 100 ÷ 50 = 2 urinals

Women:
- Toilets: 50 ÷ 25 = 2 toilets

TOTAL: 6 toilets (4 male, 2 female) + 2 urinals (male)

CLEANLINESS:
- Cleaned twice per 8-hour shift (morning, afternoon)
- Soap, hand wash provided
- Sanitary napkin vending machines (for women, mandatory if >30 female workers)
```

#### 5.3 Canteen (Section 46)
```
MANDATORY IF:
≥250 workers employed

REQUIREMENTS:

1. LOCATION:
   - Separate building or partitioned area (not in production zone)
   - Adequate seating (chairs, benches for 30% workers at a time, rotating batches)
   - Ventilation, lighting

2. FOOD:
   - Wholesome, nutritious, affordable
   - Subsidized (employer bears ≥50% cost)
   - Example: Meal costs ₹80 to prepare → Worker pays ₹40, employer subsidizes ₹40

3. HYGIENE:
   - Food handlers: Medical fitness certificates (annually)
   - Kitchen: Clean, pest-free, separate storage for dry/wet/cold items
   - Utensils washed with hot water (sanitized)

4. MANAGEMENT:
   - Canteen Committee: 50% employer reps, 50% worker reps (elected)
   - Monthly meetings (menu, pricing, hygiene complaints)
   - Accounts audited (subsidy verification)

5. TIMINGS:
   - Open during all shifts (24/7 if factory runs 3 shifts)
   - Lunch break: 30-45 minutes (as per shift timings)
```

#### 5.4 Crèche (Section 48)
```
MANDATORY IF:
≥30 female workers employed

REQUIREMENTS:

1. LOCATION:
   - Within factory premises or nearby (max 500 meters)
   - Separate room (not in production area)
   - Area: ≥15 sq meters (for up to 10 children), +1 sq meter per additional child

2. FACILITIES:
   - Clean, ventilated, well-lit
   - Drinking water, washing facilities
   - Suitable toys, play materials
   - First aid kit

3. STAFFING:
   - Trained attendants (1 per 10 children)
   - Qualification: Montessori training or child-care certification

4. TIMINGS:
   - Open during all working hours (including overtime)
   - Mothers allowed 2 breaks per day (15 minutes each) for nursing (paid)

5. FREE SERVICE:
   - No charge to workers for crèche use

EXAMPLE:
Factory employs 80 female workers, 25 have children <6 years

Crèche capacity: Design for 30 children (buffer for future)
Area: 15 + (30-10) = 35 sq meters
Attendants: 30 ÷ 10 = 3 attendants
Employer's annual cost: ₹3 lakh (attendant salaries ₹60,000/year × 3, infrastructure, supplies)
```

#### 5.5 First Aid (Section 45)
```
MANDATORY (all factories):
- First aid boxes: 1 per 150 workers
- Contents:
  - Antiseptic solution (Dettol, Savlon)
  - Bandages (various sizes)
  - Cotton wool, gauze
  - Adhesive plasters (Band-Aid)
  - Burn ointment
  - Scissors, tweezers, safety pins
  - Pain relievers (Paracetamol tablets)
  - Snake bite serum (if factory in rural/forested area)

- First-aiders: 1 trained person per shift (St. John Ambulance certification or equivalent)

ADDITIONAL (for factories with >500 workers):
- Ambulance room (separate, equipped with stretcher, examination table)
- Qualified nurse (full-time, nursing degree)
- Medical Officer (part-time visit, 2 hours/day minimum)

ACCIDENTS:
- Serious accidents (fractures, burns, electric shock): Rush to hospital (ambulance or company vehicle)
- First-aider provides immediate care (stop bleeding, CPR if needed)
- Accident register maintained (Form 36)
```

---

### 6. WORKING HOURS & OVERTIME (Chapter VI, Section 51-65)

#### 6.1 Maximum Working Hours (Section 51, 54)
```
DAILY LIMIT:
- 9 hours per day (for adults)
- Cannot exceed 10 hours including overtime (rare exceptions)

WEEKLY LIMIT:
- 48 hours per week (regular)
- Cannot exceed 60 hours including overtime

INTERVALS (Section 55):
- Minimum 30 minutes rest after 5 hours of continuous work
- Meal break (unpaid, workers can leave workstation)

WEEKLY OFF (Section 52):
- One full day (24 hours) per week
- Usually Sunday (or any fixed day)
- Compensatory off within 30 days if weekly off worked (for emergency production)

SPREAD OVER (Section 56):
- Total span from start to end of shift: Max 10.5 hours (including breaks)
- Example: Shift 8 AM to 6 PM (10 hours) → Work 9 hours, break 1 hour → Compliant
- If shift exceeds 10.5 hours → Violation (even if actual work is 9 hours)
```

#### 6.2 Overtime (Section 59)
```
CALCULATION:
Overtime = Hours worked beyond 9 hours/day OR 48 hours/week

OVERTIME WAGES:
Double the ordinary rate (Factories Act mandates 2x, unlike ID Act which allows 2x)

EXAMPLE:
Worker's monthly salary: ₹26,000 (for 26 working days, 8 hours/day)
Hourly rate: ₹26,000 ÷ (26 days × 8 hours) = ₹125/hour
Overtime rate: ₹125 × 2 = ₹250/hour

In a month, worker does:
- Regular: 208 hours (26 days × 8 hours)
- Overtime: 20 hours (some days worked 9-10 hours)

Overtime pay: 20 hours × ₹250 = ₹5,000
Total salary: ₹26,000 + ₹5,000 = ₹31,000

ANNUAL LIMIT (Section 64):
No worker shall work >60 hours overtime in any quarter (3 months)
If urgent: State government may allow exemption (by notification, temporary)
```

#### 6.3 Night Shifts (Section 66)
```
PROHIBITION FOR WOMEN (Section 66):
- Women cannot work between 7 PM and 6 AM (general rule)
- Exception: State government may allow (with safeguards)
  - Adequate lighting, safe transport home
  - Crèche operational during night shift
  - Written consent of women workers

RECENT AMENDMENT (2019):
- Many states now allow women night shifts (after Supreme Court judgment, gender equality)
- Conditions:
  - Employer provides transport (drop at doorstep, not bus stop)
  - Security guard in factory during night
  - Separate rest room for women
  - CCTV surveillance (factory premises)

CHILDREN (14-18 years):
- Prohibited from night work (7 PM to 6 AM) - NO EXCEPTIONS
- Maximum 4.5 hours/day work allowed
- Not during school hours (if enrolled in school)
```

---

### 7. LEAVE PROVISIONS (Section 79)

#### 7.1 Earned Leave (Annual Leave with Wages)
```
ELIGIBILITY:
Worker who has worked ≥240 days in a calendar year

ACCRUAL FORMULA:
1 day of earned leave for every 20 days worked

CALCULATION:
Worker worked 280 days in 2024
Earned leave accrued: 280 ÷ 20 = 14 days

ACCUMULATION:
- Maximum: 30 days (some states allow 45 days, check state rules)
- Carry forward to next year (till maximum reached)

ENCASHMENT:
- On resignation, retirement, termination: All accumulated leave must be encashed
- Payment: Leave days × (Basic salary ÷ 30 days)

CANNOT BE DENIED:
- If worker applies for earned leave (min 7 days notice), employer must grant
- Exception: Production emergency (employer can request postponement by 2 weeks max)

EXAMPLE:
Worker has 20 days earned leave balance
Applies for 10 days leave (for family function)
Employer must grant (cannot deny citing work pressure)

Leave encashment on resignation:
Remaining 10 days + accrued leave in final year
If Basic = ₹30,000/month
Encashment = 10 × (₹30,000 ÷ 30) = ₹10,000
```

---

### 8. INSPECTIONS & PENALTIES

#### 8.1 Factory Inspector Powers (Section 9-10)
```
INSPECTION AUTHORITY:
- State Factory Inspectors (appointed by State Government)
- Chief Inspector of Factories (state-level, heads the department)

POWERS (Section 9):
1. ENTRY AT ANY TIME:
   - Unannounced visits (daytime or night shift, if factory operates 24/7)
   - No prior notice required

2. EXAMINATION:
   - Inspect machinery, safety guards, fire equipment
   - Check registers (wage, attendance, leave, accidents)
   - Measure air quality, noise levels, lighting

3. SAMPLES:
   - Take samples (raw materials, finished products, air/water)
   - Test for hazardous substances (in certified labs)

4. INTERVIEW WORKERS:
   - Privately (without employer present)
   - Ask about wages, working hours, safety concerns

5. SEIZE DOCUMENTS:
   - If violations suspected (false registers, non-payment of wages)
   - Issue receipt, return after investigation

OBSTRUCTION OF INSPECTOR (Section 96):
CRIMINAL OFFENSE:
- Refusing entry → Imprisonment up to 6 months + Fine
- Destroying evidence → Imprisonment up to 2 years + Fine

COMPLIANCE:
Employer must cooperate (provide requested documents, access, answers)
```

#### 8.2 Penalties (Section 92-97)
```
MINOR VIOLATIONS (Section 92):
Examples: Dirty floors, insufficient drinking water, broken toilet flush
Penalty: Fine up to ₹1,00,000 (first offense)
        Fine up to ₹2,00,000 (repeat offense within 2 years)

MAJOR VIOLATIONS (Section 92):
Examples: No fencing on machinery, inadequate fire exits, overworked employees
Penalty: Imprisonment up to 2 years + Fine up to ₹2,00,000
        (If violation causes accident: Imprisonment up to 5 years)

ACCIDENT CAUSING DEATH (Section 88, 89):
If non-compliance with Act causes worker's death:
Penalty: Imprisonment up to 2 years (simple or rigorous) + Fine
        Criminal case (besides civil compensation to family)

FAILURE TO SEND ACCIDENT NOTICE (Section 88):
If serious accident (death, serious bodily injury) occurs:
- Employer must notify Inspector within 24 hours
- Failure to notify: Fine up to ₹50,000

CONTINUING OFFENSE (Section 95):
If violation continues despite notice from Inspector:
- Additional fine: ₹1,000 per day for each day violation continues
- Example: Unfenced machinery (Inspector notices, issues 30-day rectification notice)
  If not rectified in 30 days: Base fine ₹2L + (₹1,000 × days of delay)
```

---

## Integration with Other Protocols

### Cross-References
```
RELATED PROTOCOLS:

1. IL-LABOUR-LEAVE-ENTITLEMENTS
   - Factories Act Section 79 (earned leave accrual, encashment)
   - Integration with casual/sick leave (state S&E Acts if applicable)

2. IL-LABOUR-EMPLOYMENT-AGREEMENT
   - Working hours clause (48 hours/week, Factories Act compliance)
   - Overtime clause (2x ordinary rate, Section 59)
   - Safety obligations (employer's duty, worker's duty to use PPE)

3. IL-LABOUR-TERMINATION-FOR-CAUSE
   - Violation of safety rules (refusal to wear PPE) = misconduct
   - Dismissal justified if endangers self or others

4. IL-LABOUR-MINIMUM-WAGES
   - Factories Act working hours + Minimum Wages Act rates
   - Overtime calculation (2x minimum wage if applicable)

5. IL-LABOUR-INDUSTRIAL-DISPUTES
   - Disputes over working conditions (safety, cleanliness, canteen quality)
   - Conciliation/adjudication on Factories Act violations

6. IL-LABOUR-SHOPS-ESTABLISHMENTS (next protocol)
   - Complementary coverage (factories vs shops/offices)
   - If establishment has both factory and office: Both Acts apply to respective areas
```

---

## Compliance Checklist

### For Factory Owners/Managers

**Registration & Licensing:**
- [ ] Register factory (60 days before start, Form 1)
- [ ] Obtain license (if hazardous process, Form 7, annual renewal)
- [ ] Display registration certificate (at factory entrance)
- [ ] Notify changes (new process, increased workers, change of manager - Form 3)

**Health Provisions:**
- [ ] Daily cleaning (floors, machinery, whitewash walls annually)
- [ ] Adequate ventilation (fans, exhaust for toxic fumes)
- [ ] Temperature control (air coolers if hot processes)
- [ ] Dust/fume extractors (at source machines)
- [ ] Lighting: ≥200 lux factory floor, ≥500 lux precision work
- [ ] Overcrowding: ≥14.2 cubic meters per worker

**Safety Provisions:**
- [ ] Fence all moving machinery (gears, belts, shafts, blades)
- [ ] Emergency stop buttons (tested monthly)
- [ ] Trained operators only (for dangerous machines)
- [ ] Annual inspection (pressure vessels, lifting equipment)
- [ ] Fire safety: 2 exits/floor, extinguishers (1 per 100 sq m), smoke detectors
- [ ] Fire drill: Twice per year, documented

**Welfare Provisions:**
- [ ] Drinking water: Marked points, 5 liters/worker/day, annual water testing
- [ ] Toilets: 1 per 25 workers (separate male/female), cleaned twice per shift
- [ ] Urinals (men): 1 per 50 male workers
- [ ] Canteen (if ≥250 workers): Subsidized food, canteen committee, hygiene
- [ ] Crèche (if ≥30 female workers): 15+ sq meters, trained attendants, free
- [ ] First aid: 1 box per 150 workers, trained first-aider per shift

**Working Hours:**
- [ ] Max 9 hours/day, 48 hours/week (regular)
- [ ] Overtime: Max 60 hours/quarter, paid at 2x rate
- [ ] Breaks: 30 min after 5 hours work
- [ ] Weekly off: 1 day (24 hours) per week, compensatory if worked
- [ ] Women night shifts: Only if state permits + transport + safety measures
- [ ] Children (14-18): No night work, max 4.5 hours/day

**Leave:**
- [ ] Earned leave: 1 day per 20 worked, max 30 days accumulation
- [ ] Grant leave when applied (min 7 days notice)
- [ ] Encash on exit (all accumulated leave)

**Registers & Records (maintain for 5 years):**
- [ ] Muster roll (daily attendance)
- [ ] Wage register
- [ ] Leave register (Form 12)
- [ ] Overtime register
- [ ] Accident register (Form 36)
- [ ] Inspection book (Inspector's visit notes)

**Annual Submissions:**
- [ ] Annual return to Inspector (by 31st January, covering previous year)
- [ ] License renewal application (30 days before expiry, if hazardous)

---

## Common Pitfalls

### For Employers
1. **Operating without registration**: Criminal offense. Register 60 days before start (Form 1).

2. **Inadequate fire safety**: Major violation. Ensure 2 exits/floor, extinguishers, fire drill twice/year.

3. **Overtime exceeding limits**: 60 hours/quarter maximum. Track monthly, enforce limits (or seek govt exemption for emergencies).

4. **No crèche despite ≥30 female workers**: Mandatory. Cannot claim "no demand from workers" (legal obligation, not preference).

5. **Unfenced machinery**: If accident occurs, criminal liability (imprisonment up to 5 years). Fence ALL moving parts.

### For Workers
1. **Ignoring safety gear**: Refusing to wear PPE (helmets, goggles, gloves) = misconduct. Employer can dismiss after warning.

2. **Not reporting accidents**: Serious accident → Inform supervisor immediately. First aid, hospital, compensation claim.

3. **Accepting less overtime pay**: Factories Act mandates 2x rate. If paid 1.5x or 1x, file complaint with Inspector.

4. **Not utilizing leave**: Earned leave lapses after maximum accumulation (30 days). Use leave periodically (health, rest).

---

## Conclusion

The Factories Act 1948 is the cornerstone of industrial safety and welfare in India, protecting lakhs of factory workers from hazardous conditions. Key takeaways:

1. **Applicability**: ≥10 workers (with power) or ≥20 workers (without power) + manufacturing process.

2. **Dual Compliance**: Registration (all factories) + License (hazardous processes like chemicals, explosives).

3. **Health**: Cleanliness, ventilation, temperature, dust control, no overcrowding (14.2 cubic meters/worker).

4. **Safety**: Fencing of machinery, fire safety (2 exits, extinguishers, drills), emergency stop buttons, trained operators.

5. **Welfare**: Drinking water, toilets (1 per 25), canteen (≥250 workers), crèche (≥30 women), first aid (1 per 150).

6. **Working Hours**: 48 hours/week, 9 hours/day max, overtime at 2x rate, weekly off, earned leave (1 day per 20 worked).

7. **Inspections**: Unannounced, anytime, wide powers (entry, examination, samples, interviews, seizure).

8. **Penalties**: ₹1-2 lakh fines (minor), imprisonment up to 2-5 years (major, if causes accident/death).

Compliance is not optional—it is legally mandated and strictly enforced. The cost of non-compliance (fines, imprisonment, license revocation, worker injuries, death) far outweighs the cost of safety equipment, welfare facilities, and regulatory compliance. Progressive employers view Factories Act compliance not as burden, but as investment in worker safety, productivity, and long-term business sustainability.

---
