## Landmark Cases

### 1. Booz Allen & Hamilton v. SBI (Delhi High Court, 2011)

**Facts**:
- Booz Allen (management consultancy) issued offer letter to candidate (₹25 lakh CTC, joining date 1st June)
- Candidate accepted offer, resigned from SBI (served 2-month notice, forewent SBI bonus)
- 1 week before joining, Booz Allen withdrew offer (cited "business reasons" - recession, project cancellations)
- Candidate sued for damages (₹15 lakh = foregone SBI bonus + 6 months salary loss)

**Issue**: Is offer letter binding on employer? Can employer withdraw offer after acceptance without liability?

**Held**:
1. **Offer Letter = Binding Contract**: Once candidate accepts offer, it becomes enforceable contract (Indian Contract Act Section 2(h))
2. **Withdrawal = Breach**: Employer's withdrawal after acceptance is breach of contract (unless offer explicitly conditional and conditions not met)
3. **Damages Payable**: Employer liable for:
   - Foregone salary/bonus from previous employer (candidate resigned in reliance on offer)
   - Reasonable job search period (6 months salary awarded as candidate unemployed for 6 months post-withdrawal)
   - Actual damages (not punitive): Court awarded ₹12 lakh (₹5L foregone SBI bonus + ₹7L salary for 6 months job search)

**Principle**:
- Employers CANNOT casually withdraw offers after acceptance (even if offer states "at-will employment")
- "Business reasons" NOT sufficient justification unless offer explicitly conditional on budget approval, project sanction, etc.
- **Best Practice**: Make offers explicitly conditional ("subject to Board approval", "subject to project go-ahead") if business uncertainty exists

### 2. Percept D'Mark (India) Pvt Ltd v. Zaheer Khan (Bombay High Court, 2006)

**Facts**:
- Zaheer Khan (cricketer) signed exclusive endorsement contract with Percept D'Mark (3 years, ₹1.8 crore, non-compete with rival brands)
- 18 months later, Zaheer signed with Reebok (competing sportswear brand) despite non-compete
- Percept sued for injunction (restrain Zaheer from endorsing Reebok)

**Issue**: Are non-compete clauses enforceable in employment/service contracts? (Section 27 Indian Contract Act states "restraint of trade" void)

**Held**:
1. **Non-Compete Valid if Reasonable**: Section 27 voids restraints of trade, BUT reasonable restrictions protecting legitimate business interests are enforceable
2. **Reasonableness Test**: 3-factor test applied
   - Duration: 18 months remaining (out of original 3 years) - Reasonable (not perpetual)
   - Geography: Nationwide (India only) - Reasonable (Zaheer's endorsements valuable only in India, not global restriction)
   - Scope: Sportswear/sports equipment brands only (not all brands) - Reasonable (narrow scope, protects Percept's specific business interest)
3. **Injunction Granted**: Zaheer restrained from endorsing competing brands for remaining 18 months

**Principle**:
- Non-compete clauses in offer letters/employment agreements are enforceable if:
  - Duration reasonable (6-24 months post-exit, NOT lifetime ban)
  - Geography reasonable (city/state/country where employee worked, NOT worldwide)
  - Scope reasonable (specific industry/clients, NOT all employment)
- Protects employer's legitimate interests: Confidential information, client relationships, trade secrets

---

## Compliance Checklist

### For Employers (HR Drafting Offer Letter)

- [ ] Verify CTC structure complies with minimum wage laws (Basic ≥50% of CTC if covered by Minimum Wages Act)
- [ ] Ensure EPF applicability checked (establishment ≥20 employees → EPF mandatory, employer contribution 12%)
- [ ] ESIC applicability checked (establishment ≥10 employees in some states, wage ≤₹21,000/month → ESIC mandatory, employer contribution 3.25%)
- [ ] Probation period within legal limits (6 months for workmen per Standing Orders Act, 12 months for managers/supervisors)
- [ ] Notice period realistic (14-90 days, avoid "at-will employment" clause)
- [ ] Background verification clause includes consent for data sharing (DPDP Act 2023 compliance)
- [ ] Medical examination clause specifies company-appointed doctor (avoids disputes on fitness)
- [ ] Non-compete clause reasonable in duration, geography, scope (avoid blanket bans, Section 27 Indian Contract Act)
- [ ] Performance bonus criteria clear (payout %, rating matrix, proration for part-year)
- [ ] Offer validity and acceptance timeline specified (avoid indefinite offers)

### For Candidates (Reviewing Offer Letter)

- [ ] CTC breakdown transparent (Basic, HRA, Special Allowance, employer PF clearly mentioned)
- [ ] Monthly take-home calculated (not just annual CTC, account for PF, TDS, professional tax deductions)
- [ ] Joining date feasible (considering current employer's notice period, relocation time)
- [ ] Probation terms acceptable (6 months standard, 14-30 days notice during probation)
- [ ] Notice period manageable (90 days standard for senior roles, 30-60 days for junior roles)
- [ ] Background verification scope reasonable (education, employment, criminal - NOT social media, caste/religion checks)
- [ ] Non-compete clause reasonable (12-24 months post-exit acceptable, lifetime ban challenge in court)
- [ ] Restrictive covenants acceptable (confidentiality perpetual OK, non-solicitation 12 months OK)
- [ ] Benefits clearly mentioned (leave, insurance, laptop, learning budget)
- [ ] Acceptance communicated within timeline (7 days standard, request extension if needed)

## IL-LABOUR-PAYMENT-OF-WAGES.md
---

# IL-LABOUR-PAYMENT-OF-WAGES

## Protocol ID
IL-LABOUR-PAYMENT-OF-WAGES

## Classification
- **Domain**: Labour Law
- **Sub-domain**: Statutory Compliance
- **Type**: Operational Protocol
- **Complexity**: Medium
- **Version**: 1.0.0

---

## Purpose
This protocol provides comprehensive guidance on the Payment of Wages Act 1936, covering timely wage payment, permissible and prohibited deductions, wage payment methods, delayed payment penalties, claims procedures, and enforcement mechanisms.

---

## Core Framework

### 1. APPLICABILITY (Section 1)

#### 1.1 Coverage
```
PAYMENT OF WAGES ACT 1936:

APPLIES TO:
✓ Factories (under Factories Act 1948)
✓ Industrial establishments (employing ≥10 workers with power, ≥20 without power)
✓ Railways
✓ Tramways, motor transport service
✓ Air transport service
✓ Docks, wharves, inland vessels
✓ Plantations
✓ Workshops, establishments notified by State Government

WAGE LIMIT (originally):
Act applied only to workers earning ≤₹24,000/month
Amendment 2017: Wage ceiling REMOVED
Now applies to ALL employees (regardless of wage level)

EMPLOYEE DEFINITION (Section 2(1)):
Person employed for hire/reward to do skilled, unskilled, manual, clerical, supervisory work
Includes: Workmen, clerks, supervisors (earning salary, not managerial)
Excludes: Managerial, administrative staff (not on wage/salary basis, partnership, directors)
```

---

### 2. WAGE PAYMENT TIMELINES (Section 5)

#### 2.1 Payment Frequency & Deadlines
```
ESTABLISHMENTS WITH <1,000 EMPLOYEES:
Must pay wages by 7th of following month

EXAMPLE:
Factory with 500 workers
Salary month: January 2025 (1st Jan to 31st Jan)
Payment deadline: 7th February 2025 (latest)

If paid on 8th Feb → VIOLATION (delay penalty applicable)

ESTABLISHMENTS WITH ≥1,000 EMPLOYEES:
Must pay wages by 10th of following month

EXAMPLE:
IT company with 2,500 employees
Salary month: March 2025
Payment deadline: 10th April 2025

WEEKLY/DAILY WAGE WORKERS:
If wages calculated weekly/daily (not monthly)
→ Must pay before expiry of 7th day from end of wage period

EXAMPLE:
Construction workers (weekly wages, Monday to Sunday)
Week ending: 15th December 2024 (Sunday)
Payment deadline: 22nd December 2024 (7th day from 15th)

TERMINATION/RESIGNATION:
Wages due on exit (Full & Final settlement)
→ Must pay within 2 working days from termination date

EXAMPLE:
Employee resigns: 20th January 2025 (last working day)
F&F settlement: Salary (20 days of Jan) + Leave encashment + Notice pay (if applicable)
Payment deadline: 22nd January 2025 (2 working days)
```

---

### 3. MODE OF WAGE PAYMENT (Section 6)

#### 3.1 Currency & Methods
```
PRIMARY RULE (Section 6(1)):
Wages shall be paid in CURRENCY COINS or CURRENCY NOTES (cash)

MODERN AMENDMENT (2017):
Wages can be paid by:
1. Cash (physical currency)
2. Cheque (bank cheque, drawn on worker's bank)
3. Direct bank credit (NEFT, RTGS, IMPS to worker's account)

MANDATORY FOR WAGES ≥₹1,000/month (or as notified):
Payment through bank account COMPULSORY
(Anti-corruption measure, digital India initiative)

WORKER'S CONSENT:
For electronic payment (cheque/bank transfer), worker's written consent required
Employer must obtain: Bank account details, authorization to credit salary

WAGE SLIP MANDATORY (not in Act, but standard practice + some state rules):
Employer must provide wage slip showing:
- Gross salary (Basic + DA + HRA + Allowances + Bonus)
- Deductions (PF, ESI, PT, TDS, Loans, Advances)
- Net salary (credited to bank)
- Payment month

EXAMPLE WAGE SLIP:
Employee: XYZ
Month: January 2025
EARNINGS:
Basic Salary: ₹18,000
DA: ₹5,400 (30%)
HRA: ₹9,000 (50%)
Transport: ₹2,500
GROSS: ₹34,900

DEDUCTIONS:
PF (Employee 12%): ₹1,800 (on ₹15,000 Basic, capped)
ESI (Employee 0.75%): ₹262
Professional Tax: ₹200
TDS: ₹150
Loan EMI: ₹2,000
TOTAL DEDUCTIONS: ₹4,412

NET SALARY: ₹30,488 (credited to bank on 7th Feb)
```

---

### 4. PERMISSIBLE DEDUCTIONS (Section 7)

#### 4.1 Categories of Deductions
```
ONLY FOLLOWING DEDUCTIONS ALLOWED (Section 7):

1. FINES (Section 7(2)(a)):
   - For misconduct, rule violation (as per Standing Orders or employment contract)
   - Conditions:
     - Fine must be mentioned in Standing Orders (approved by Labour Dept)
     - Worker given opportunity to explain (show cause notice)
     - Fine ≤3% of wages per month (maximum cap)
     - Fine credited to welfare fund (not employer's pocket)

   EXAMPLE:
   Worker late for 5 days (fine: ₹100/day per Standing Orders)
   Monthly wages: ₹25,000
   Maximum fine allowed: 3% × ₹25,000 = ₹750
   Actual fine: 5 × ₹100 = ₹500 (within limit, permissible)

2. ABSENCE FROM DUTY (Section 7(2)(b)):
   - Deduction for days absent (proportionate to wages)
   - Formula: (Monthly wages ÷ 26 days) × Absent days

   EXAMPLE:
   Monthly salary: ₹30,000
   Absent: 3 days (unauthorized leave)
   Deduction: (₹30,000 ÷ 26) × 3 = ₹3,462

3. ACCOMMODATION PROVIDED BY EMPLOYER (Section 7(2)(c)):
   - If employer provides housing (quarters, dormitory)
   - Deduction for rent (but ≤ fair market rent, not excessive)

   EXAMPLE:
   Company provides 2BHK flat (market rent ₹15,000/month)
   Deduction from salary: ₹10,000/month (subsidized, within fair value)

4. AMENITIES & SERVICES (Section 7(2)(d)):
   - Electricity, water, conservancy (if employer provides)
   - Transport (if company bus provided)
   - Medical facilities (if company clinic/insurance)
   - Canteen (subsidized meals, worker pays part)

   CONDITION: Deduction ≤ actual cost to employer (no profit)

5. DAMAGE OR LOSS (Section 7(2)(e)):
   - Damage to goods, tools, machinery (due to worker's negligence)
   - Lost items (tools, materials entrusted to worker)

   CONDITIONS:
   - Employer must prove negligence (not normal wear & tear)
   - Worker given opportunity to explain (domestic inquiry)
   - Deduction ≤ actual loss amount
   - Cannot exceed 50% of wages in any month (to ensure living wage)

   EXAMPLE:
   Worker damages machine (negligence, not operational failure)
   Repair cost: ₹25,000
   Worker's monthly wage: ₹20,000
   Deduction: ₹10,000/month (50% max) × 2.5 months = ₹25,000 recovered over time

6. HOUSE-BUILDING, FAMILY PENSION FUND (Section 7(2)(f)):
   - Contribution to approved funds (EPF, insurance, pension)
   - Worker's written authorization required

7. COOPERATIVE SOCIETIES (Section 7(2)(g)):
   - Loan repayment to cooperative society (if worker is member)
   - Deduction as per society rules
   - Written authorization

8. INSURANCE PREMIUM (Section 7(2)(h)):
   - Group insurance, medical insurance (if employer provides)
   - Worker's consent + benefit accrues to worker/family

9. ADVANCES & LOANS (Section 7(2)(j)):
   - Salary advance repayment (taken by worker)
   - Loan EMI (from employer or bank, authorized by worker)

   EXAMPLE:
   Worker takes ₹50,000 salary advance (personal emergency)
   Repayment: ₹5,000/month × 10 months (deducted from salary)

10. INCOME TAX (Section 7(2)(i)):
    - TDS (Tax Deducted at Source)
    - As per Income Tax Act (employer's statutory obligation)
    - Deposited to govt, Form 16 issued to worker

11. PROFESSIONAL TAX (state-specific):
    - State government levy (Maharashtra, Karnataka, etc.)
    - ₹200-₹2,500/month (based on salary slab)
    - Employer deducts, deposits to state

12. COURT ORDERS (Section 7(2)(k)):
    - Attachment of wages (court order in civil suit)
    - Maintenance to spouse/children (family court order)
    - Employer must comply (legal obligation)

    LIMIT: Maximum 50% of wages can be attached (protect worker's subsistence)
```

#### 4.2 Prohibited Deductions
```
FOLLOWING DEDUCTIONS NOT ALLOWED:

✗ Deduction for employer's business loss (market downturn, profit loss)
✗ Deduction for tools, machinery (employer must provide free)
✗ Deduction for safety equipment (helmets, gloves, shoes - employer provides)
✗ Deduction for recruitment expenses (agent fees, training cost before joining)
✗ Deduction for employer's charity, donations (without worker's voluntary consent)
✗ Arbitrary deductions (without written agreement, standing orders, or statutory basis)

IF EMPLOYER MAKES PROHIBITED DEDUCTION:
Worker files complaint → Inspector investigates
If proved: Employer must refund amount + penalty (fine ₹7,500-₹50,000)

CASE EXAMPLE (Supreme Court - People's Union for Democratic Rights, 1982):
Employer deducted ₹500/month for "welfare fund" (not defined, no benefit shown)
Court: Illegal deduction, refund all workers, fine ₹5 lakh (at that time, significant)
```

---

### 5. DELAYED PAYMENT - COMPENSATION (Section 15, Rule 26)

#### 5.1 Penalty for Delay
```
EMPLOYER'S DUTY:
Pay wages by due date (7th/10th of following month, or 2 days on termination)

IF DELAYED:
Employer liable to pay COMPENSATION to worker

COMPENSATION RATE (Central Rules, varies by state):
- Delhi, Maharashtra: 0.05% per day of delayed amount
- Karnataka: 0.5% per day (more stringent)
- Generally: Interest-like compensation (not fixed in Act, rules specify)

CALCULATION EXAMPLE (Maharashtra):
Salary: ₹30,000
Due date: 7th Feb 2025
Actual payment: 25th Feb 2025 (18 days delay)
Delay compensation: ₹30,000 × 0.05% × 18 days = ₹270

Worker entitled to: ₹30,000 + ₹270 = ₹30,270

CRIMINAL LIABILITY (Section 20):
Willful delay (without reasonable cause)
→ Fine ₹7,500-₹50,000 (on employer)
→ Prosecution in magistrate court

REASONABLE CAUSE (defense):
- Bank strike, system failure (force majeure)
- Worker's bank account closed/frozen (not employer's fault)
- Natural calamity (floods, earthquake affecting operations)

If reasonable cause proved: No penalty (but wages still due)
```

---

### 6. CLAIMS & ENFORCEMENT

#### 6.1 Authority Under Act (Section 15)
```
AUTHORITY:
Appointed by State Government (usually Labour Commissioner or Inspector)

JURISDICTION:
Territorial (each district has Authority)
Worker files claim in district where employed
```

#### 6.2 Claims Procedure (Section 15)
```
WHO CAN FILE:
- Worker (individual)
- Legal heir (if worker deceased, unpaid wages)
- Assignee (if worker assigned claim rights to union/legal aid)
- Inspector (on behalf of workers, if systemic non-payment)

TIME LIMIT (Section 15(2)):
Within 12 months from date wage became due

EXAMPLE:
Salary for Jan 2025, due on 7th Feb 2025 (not paid)
Claim can be filed till 7th Feb 2026 (12 months from due date)

After 12 months: Claim time-barred (Authority cannot entertain)
Exception: If delay condoned (reasonable cause, Authority's discretion)

PROCEDURE:

STEP 1: APPLICATION (Form A or as per state rules)
Details:
- Worker's name, address, designation
- Employer's name, establishment details
- Wage period, amount due
- Date wage became due, date of claim
- Reasons for non-payment (if known)

STEP 2: NOTICE TO EMPLOYER
Authority issues notice (15-30 days to reply)

STEP 3: EMPLOYER'S REPLY
Defenses:
- "Wage already paid" (proof: bank statement, payroll, acknowledgment)
- "Worker not entitled" (terminated before wage period, suspended)
- "Calculation wrong" (disputes gross/deductions)

STEP 4: INQUIRY (if disputed)
- Evidence: Wage slips, bank statements, employment contract
- Witnesses: Supervisor, HR manager, fellow workers
- Documents: Attendance records, resignation letter, F&F statement

STEP 5: ORDER (Section 15(4))
Authority passes order:
- "Employer shall pay ₹[X] to worker within 30 days"
- Interest/compensation for delay (if applicable)
- Costs (₹[Y], if employer's resistance frivolous)

STEP 6: EXECUTION (if employer does not pay)
Order sent to Labour Court → Executed as decree
- Attachment of bank accounts
- Recovery from employer's assets

TIMELINE:
Application → Order: 3-6 months (if no complications)
```

---

### 7. REGISTERS & RECORDS (Section 13)

#### 7.1 Mandatory Registers
```
EVERY EMPLOYER MUST MAINTAIN:

1. REGISTER OF WAGES (Form I or equivalent):
   Columns:
   - Employee name, designation, employee ID
   - Wage period (monthly, weekly, daily)
   - Gross wages (Basic + DA + HRA + Allowances)
   - Deductions (PF, ESI, PT, TDS, Fines, Advances, Loans)
   - Net wages paid
   - Date of payment
   - Mode of payment (cash/cheque no./bank credit)
   - Employee's signature or thumb impression (acknowledging receipt)

2. REGISTER OF FINES (if fines imposed):
   - Employee name
   - Date of offense, nature of offense
   - Show cause notice date, employee's explanation
   - Fine amount imposed
   - Date fine deducted from wages
   - Disposal of fine (welfare fund, not employer's income)

3. REGISTER OF DEDUCTIONS FOR DAMAGE/LOSS:
   - Employee name
   - Nature of damage/loss (machine broken, material lost)
   - Amount of damage (assessed value)
   - Inquiry conducted (domestic inquiry report)
   - Employee's explanation
   - Deduction amount, installments

4. MUSTER ROLL (if daily/weekly wages):
   - Employee name
   - Daily attendance (present, absent, leave, half-day)
   - Hours worked (if hourly wages)
   - Overtime hours (if applicable)

RECORD RETENTION:
Maintain for 3 years from last entry
Must be produced to Inspector on demand

DIGITAL REGISTERS:
Permitted (Excel, HRMS software, cloud-based)
Provided: Easily accessible, printable, tamper-proof (audit trail)
```

---

### 8. INSPECTIONS & PENALTIES

#### 8.1 Inspector's Powers (Section 13)
```
INSPECTOR (appointed by State Government):

POWERS:
1. Enter establishment (during working hours, without prior notice)
2. Examine wage registers, muster rolls, deduction registers
3. Interview employees (about wages, deductions, delays)
4. Seize documents (if violations suspected, false records)
5. File prosecution (if systematic violations, willful non-payment)

EMPLOYER'S DUTY:
Cooperate (provide registers, answer questions, allow entry)

OBSTRUCTION:
Refusing entry, destroying records → Fine ₹50,000 + Imprisonment up to 6 months
```

#### 8.2 Penalties (Section 20-22)
```
DELAYED PAYMENT (without reasonable cause):
Fine: ₹7,500 to ₹50,000 (per violation)
Prosecution: In magistrate court
Imprisonment: Not specified (only fine in most cases)

UNAUTHORIZED DEDUCTIONS:
Fine: ₹7,500 to ₹50,000
Refund amount deducted illegally (to worker)

NOT MAINTAINING REGISTERS:
Fine: ₹3,000 to ₹25,000 (first offense)
Fine: ₹5,000 to ₹50,000 (repeat offense within 5 years)

OBSTRUCTION OF INSPECTOR:
Fine: ₹25,000 to ₹75,000
Imprisonment: Up to 6 months (if serious obstruction, evidence destruction)

CONTINUING OFFENSE (Section 22):
If violation continues despite notice:
Additional fine: ₹500 per day for each day violation continues
(After conviction, if employer still does not comply)

COMPOUNDING (settle without trial):
Minor violations: Can be compounded (fine paid, case closed)
Inspector's discretion, Amount: ₹10,000-₹50,000 (negotiated)
```

---

## Landmark Cases

### 1. **Mackinnon Mackenzie & Co. v. Audrey D'Costa (1987) - Supreme Court**
**Facts**: Employer deducted ₹500/month for "welfare fund" without specifying benefits or maintaining fund.

**Held**:
- Deduction for "welfare" without defined scheme = Illegal (not covered by Section 7)
- Employer must refund all deducted amounts to workers
- Vague deductions not permissible (only Section 7 categories allowed)

**Impact**: Strengthened worker protection against arbitrary deductions.

---

### 2. **People's Union for Democratic Rights v. Union of India (1982) - Supreme Court**
**Facts**: Construction workers not paid minimum wages, delayed payments for months.

**Held**:
- Non-payment/delayed payment of minimum wages = Violation of constitutional rights (Article 23 - forced labour)
- Employers prosecuted under Payment of Wages Act + Minimum Wages Act
- Workers entitled to compensation for delayed payment

**Impact**: Linked wage payment to fundamental rights, enhanced enforcement.

---

### 3. **Bridge & Roof Co. v. Union of India (1963) - Supreme Court**
**Facts**: Employer delayed wage payment citing financial difficulties.

**Held**:
- Financial difficulty is NOT reasonable cause for delayed payment
- Employer must arrange finances (loans, asset sale) to pay wages on time
- Workers' livelihood cannot be jeopardized due to employer's business losses

**Impact**: Established that wages are sacrosanct, not deferrable for business reasons.

---

## Integration with Other Protocols

### Cross-References
```
RELATED PROTOCOLS:

1. IL-LABOUR-MINIMUM-WAGES
   - Wages paid must be ≥ minimum wages (Payment of Wages Act ensures timely payment)
   - Both Acts apply cumulatively (amount + timeline compliance)

2. IL-LABOUR-EPF-ESIC
   - PF, ESI deductions (Section 7(2)(f) - authorized deductions)
   - Payment of Wages Act: By 7th/10th
   - PF/ESI deposit: By 15th (different timelines, both mandatory)

3. IL-LABOUR-BONUS
   - Bonus payment (by 8 months after accounting year end)
   - Payment of Wages Act applies (must pay by specified date, in currency/bank)

4. IL-LABOUR-GRATUITY
   - Gratuity payment (within 30 days of termination)
   - Payment of Wages Act: F&F within 2 days (gratuity included in F&F)

5. IL-LABOUR-EMPLOYMENT-AGREEMENT
   - Contract specifies wage, deductions (must comply with Section 7 limits)
   - Payment mode (bank transfer with worker's consent)

6. IL-LABOUR-TERMINATION-FOR-CAUSE
   - F&F settlement (wages + leave encashment + notice pay/gratuity)
   - Payment within 2 working days (Payment of Wages Act Section 5)
```

---

## Compliance Checklist

### For Employers

**Timely Payment:**
- [ ] Pay monthly wages by 7th (if <1,000 employees) or 10th (if ≥1,000 employees)
- [ ] Pay weekly/daily wages within 7 days of wage period end
- [ ] Pay F&F settlement within 2 working days of termination
- [ ] If delayed: Pay compensation (0.05%-0.5% per day, state rules)

**Mode of Payment:**
- [ ] If wage ≥₹1,000/month: Mandatory bank transfer (NEFT/cheque)
- [ ] Obtain worker's written consent for electronic payment
- [ ] Bank account details, authorization (one-time, on joining)
- [ ] Issue wage slip (gross, deductions, net, payment mode, month)

**Permissible Deductions Only (Section 7):**
- [ ] Fines (≤3% monthly wages, as per Standing Orders, credited to welfare fund)
- [ ] Absence (proportionate, ÷26 days × absent days)
- [ ] PF, ESI, PT, TDS (statutory, worker's authorization for PF)
- [ ] Advances, loans (written authorization, repayment schedule)
- [ ] Amenities (rent, transport, canteen - ≤actual cost, no profit)
- [ ] Damage/loss (inquiry, worker's explanation, ≤50% monthly wages)
- [ ] No arbitrary deductions (must fit Section 7 categories)

**Registers (maintain for 3 years):**
- [ ] Wage register (Form I: gross, deductions, net, signature, payment date)
- [ ] Fine register (if fines imposed: offense, inquiry, amount, disposal)
- [ ] Damage/loss register (if deductions for damage: inquiry, assessment, installments)
- [ ] Muster roll (if daily/weekly wages: attendance, hours, overtime)

**Display:**
- [ ] Abstract of Payment of Wages Act (at notice board, in local language + English)
- [ ] Wage period, payment dates (so workers know when to expect salary)

**Inspector Cooperation:**
- [ ] Allow entry during working hours (no obstruction)
- [ ] Provide registers, documents when requested
- [ ] Answer questions honestly (about wage payment, deductions)

---

### For Employees

**Know Your Rights:**
- [ ] Salary due by 7th/10th of month (check employment contract, company policy)
- [ ] F&F due within 2 days of last working day (resignation, termination)
- [ ] Only Section 7 deductions allowed (verify wage slip, question unauthorized deductions)
- [ ] Delayed payment → Entitled to compensation (0.05%-0.5% per day)

**Verify Wage Slip Monthly:**
- [ ] Gross salary correct (Basic, DA, HRA, Allowances as per offer letter)
- [ ] Deductions authorized (PF, ESI, PT, TDS statutory; loans/advances with your consent)
- [ ] Net salary matches bank credit (check bank statement)
- [ ] If discrepancy: Raise with HR immediately (written email, documented)

**File Claim if Non-Payment:**
- [ ] Within 12 months of due date (time limit, don't delay)
- [ ] Application to Authority (district Labour Commissioner, Form A or state form)
- [ ] Evidence: Wage slips, bank statements, employment contract, resignation letter, email trail
- [ ] Follow up hearings (attend, provide testimony if required)

**Termination F&F:**
- [ ] Calculate dues: Salary (pro-rata for month), Leave encashment, Notice pay (if applicable), Gratuity (if eligible)
- [ ] Demand within 2 days (written email to HR, citing Payment of Wages Act Section 5)
- [ ] If not paid: File claim with Authority + complaint to Inspector (dual action)

---

## Common Pitfalls

### For Employers
1. **Delaying salary payment**: Beyond 7th/10th = compensation + penalty + prosecution risk. Automate payroll, ensure funds availability before month-end.

2. **Arbitrary deductions**: Deducting for "shrinkage", "wastage", "training cost" without Section 7 basis = Illegal. Workers file claims, you refund + fine.

3. **Not maintaining wage register**: Inspector's first check. No register = fine ₹3,000-₹25,000. Maintain digitally (Excel/HRMS), print when needed.

4. **Delaying F&F settlement**: Must pay within 2 days of termination. Cite "pending approval", "exit interview pending" = Not reasonable cause. Pay first, settle formalities later.

5. **Fines exceeding 3%**: Standing Orders allow ₹200/day fine, impose for 10 days = ₹2,000. If monthly wage ₹20,000, 3% = ₹600 max. Excess ₹1,400 illegal.

### For Employees
1. **Not checking wage slip monthly**: Unauthorized deductions continue for months/years. Verify each month, raise issues immediately (easier to resolve recent deductions).

2. **Accepting verbal assurances**: "F&F will be paid in 15 days" (verbal). Get written email (HR/manager confirmation). If not paid, written evidence for claim.

3. **Missing 12-month claim deadline**: Salary not paid for Jan 2024 (due 7th Feb 2024). File claim before 7th Feb 2025. After that, time-barred (Authority rejects).

4. **Not demanding wage slip**: Employer pays cash, no slip. Cannot prove wages/deductions later. Insist on wage slip (even if cash payment, written receipt/slip mandatory).

---

## Conclusion

The Payment of Wages Act 1936 is one of India's oldest labour laws, ensuring that workers receive their hard-earned wages without delay or unauthorized deductions. Key takeaways:

1. **Timely Payment**: By 7th (if <1,000 employees) or 10th (if ≥1,000) of following month. F&F within 2 days of termination. Delays attract compensation + penalties.

2. **Mode**: Wages ≥₹1,000/month must be paid via bank (cheque/NEFT). Cash allowed for lower wages. Worker's consent required for electronic payment.

3. **Permissible Deductions (Section 7 only)**: Fines (≤3%), Absence, Statutory (PF/ESI/PT/TDS), Advances/Loans, Amenities (rent, transport), Damage/loss (with inquiry). Total deductions ≤50% wages (protect subsistence).

4. **Prohibited**: Arbitrary deductions (employer's loss, tools, safety equipment, charity) = Illegal. Refund + penalty.

5. **Compensation for Delay**: 0.05%-0.5% per day (state rules). Not high, but symbolic + criminal prosecution risk (₹7,500-₹50,000 fine) is deterrent.

6. **Claims**: Within 12 months, to Authority (Labour Commissioner). Quick resolution (3-6 months), executable as decree.

7. **Registers**: Wage register (Form I) mandatory for 3 years. Digital acceptable (Excel, HRMS).

8. **Penalties**: ₹7,500-₹50,000 (delayed payment, unauthorized deductions), ₹3,000-₹50,000 (no registers), ₹25,000-₹75,000 + imprisonment (obstruction).

Compliance is straightforward: Pay on time (automate payroll, bank transfers), deduct only authorized amounts (Section 7 categories), maintain wage register, provide wage slips. The cost of compliance is minimal (payroll software, admin time), while cost of non-compliance (claims, penalties, prosecution, reputational damage, worker attrition) is significant. Workers' wages fund their families' survival—prompt, full payment is not a favor, it is a legal obligation and moral imperative.

---

## Version History

| Version | Date | Changes | Author |
|---------|------|---------|--------|
| 1.0.0 | 2025-12-06 | Initial protocol creation | Claude Brain |

---

## References

### Statutes
1. Payment of Wages Act 1936 (Central Act 4 of 1936)
2. Payment of Wages (Amendment) Act 2017 (wage ceiling removed)
3. Payment of Wages Rules (State-specific, e.g., Maharashtra, Karnataka, Delhi)

### Case Law
1. Mackinnon Mackenzie & Co. v. Audrey D'Costa, (1987) 2 SCC 469
2. People's Union for Democratic Rights v. Union of India, AIR 1982 SC 1473
3. Bridge & Roof Co. (India) Ltd. v. Union of India, AIR 1963 SC 1474

### Forms
1. Form A (Application for Claim - varies by state)
2. Form I (Register of Wages)

---

**END OF PROTOCOL**

## IL-LABOUR-PROBATION-CONFIRMATION.md
---

# Probation & Confirmation - Performance Evaluation Framework

## Protocol ID
**IL-LABOUR-PROBATION-CONFIRMATION** (v1.0)

---

## Purpose
Guide employers and HR professionals through the legal framework, performance evaluation process, and best practices for managing employee probation periods and confirmation under Indian labour law, covering maximum probation duration, evaluation criteria, extension procedures, confirmation process, termination rights during probation, and employee entitlements.

---

## Legal Framework

### 1. Statutory Provisions on Probation

**Industrial Employment (Standing Orders) Act 1946 - Rule 2(vi)**:
```
"A workman shall be deemed to be on probation during the period of his original
employment in the industrial establishment and such period shall not ordinarily
exceed six months."
```

**Key Statutory Requirements**:

| **Employee Category** | **Maximum Probation** | **Extension Allowed?** | **Legal Basis** |
|-----------------------|-----------------------|------------------------|-----------------|
| **Workmen** (Industrial Employment Standing Orders Act) | 6 months | Yes, up to 12 months TOTAL (with written consent + justification) | Model Standing Orders Rule 2(vi) |
| **Managers/Supervisors** (Not covered by Standing Orders) | 12 months | Yes, employer discretion (common to extend to 18 months for senior roles) | Employment contract (no statutory limit) |
| **Apprentices** (Apprentices Act 1961) | Duration of apprenticeship (6 months to 4 years depending on trade) | N/A (apprenticeship, not probation) | Apprentices Act Section 3 |

**Applicable Establishments**:
- **Standing Orders Act**: Applies to industrial establishments with ≥100 workmen (manufacturing, mining, plantations)
- **Shops & Establishments Act**: State-specific (e.g., Maharashtra allows 6-month probation for all employees, Karnataka allows 3-6 months)

### 2. Probation vs Apprenticeship vs Fixed-Term

**Key Distinctions**:

| **Aspect** | **Probation** | **Apprenticeship** | **Fixed-Term Employment** |
|------------|---------------|--------------------|-----------------------------|
| **Purpose** | Evaluate suitability for permanent role | Skill training (government-regulated) | Project-based work (definite tenure) |
| **Duration** | 6-12 months | 6 months to 4 years (trade-specific) | 12-36 months typically |
| **Wages** | Full contractual salary (per offer letter) | Stipend (prescribed rates, lower than market wages) | Full wages (equal to permanent employees) |
| **Statutory Benefits** | Full (PF, ESIC, leave, gratuity accrual) | Limited (no PF if stipend <₹15,000) | Full (PF, leave, prorated gratuity) |
| **Termination** | 14-30 days notice | Apprenticeship can be terminated only for misconduct (not performance) | Contract expires automatically (no notice required) |
| **Outcome** | Confirmation as permanent OR termination | Certificate of completion (no automatic employment) | Fixed-term ends OR converts to permanent (if extended) |

---

## Probation Period - Rights & Obligations

### 1. Employee Rights During Probation

**Full Statutory Entitlements** (Misconception: Probation employees have reduced rights):

✅ **ENTITLED TO**:
1. **Full Wages**: As per offer letter/employment agreement (no reduction allowed during probation)
2. **Provident Fund**: 12% employer + 12% employee contribution (if establishment covered by EPF Act)
3. **ESIC**: 3.25% employer + 0.75% employee (if wage ≤₹21,000/month + establishment covered)
4. **Earned Leave**: Accrues during probation (1.5 days per month for Factories Act, prorated)
   - Example: 6-month probation → 9 days earned leave accrued (can be encashed if terminated during probation or carried forward if confirmed)
5. **Casual/Sick Leave**: As per company policy (typically 7 days casual + 7 days sick per year, prorated for probation)
6. **Gratuity Accrual**: Begins from Day 1 (payable only if ≥5 years service, but accrues during probation)
7. **Bonus**: Statutory bonus (Payment of Bonus Act) applies if wage ≤₹21,000/month (prorated for probation period)
8. **Maternity Benefit**: Female employees entitled to 26 weeks paid leave (if worked ≥80 days in preceding 12 months, probation counted)

❌ **NOT ENTITLED TO**:
1. **Long Notice Period**: Shorter notice period during probation (14-30 days vs 60-90 days post-confirmation)
2. **Severance Pay**: No compensation if terminated during probation (beyond notice pay + leave encashment)
3. **Automatic Confirmation**: Confirmation is at employer's discretion based on performance (not automatic after 6 months)

### 2. Employer's Rights During Probation

**Termination Easier** (vs Post-Confirmation):
1. **Shorter Notice**: 14-30 days notice vs 60-90 days for confirmed employees
2. **No Retrenchment Compensation**: Section 25F Industrial Disputes Act (notice + 15 days wages per year of service) does NOT apply to probation termination
3. **Lower Bar for Termination**: Can terminate for unsatisfactory performance (vs post-confirmation where employer needs to prove misconduct through domestic inquiry for cause-based termination)

**Evaluation Flexibility**:
- Can extend probation if performance borderline (from 6 months to 12 months max for workmen, requires written consent)
- Can terminate without citing specific reasons (as long as notice period given)

**Example - Probation Termination**:
```
SCENARIO: Software Engineer on 6-Month Probation

Month 3 Review:
- Performance: Below expectations (code quality issues, missed 2 sprint deadlines)
- Manager feedback: Needs improvement in coding standards, time management
- Action: Verbal warning, additional training provided

Month 5 Review:
- Performance: Still below expectations (continued delays, peer feedback negative)
- HR Decision: Terminate during probation

Termination Process:
- Notice: 30 days notice (as per employment agreement)
- Payment: Full salary for 30 days + leave encashment (5 months × 1.75 days =
  8.75 days leave accrued, encashed at Basic salary)
- No severance: No additional compensation (not entitled during probation)
- No domestic inquiry: Not required for probation termination (unless Standing
  Orders mandates inquiry even for probation)

Employee Cannot Challenge:
- Courts/labour tribunals generally do NOT interfere with probation terminations
  (employer's subjective assessment of "unsatisfactory performance" respected)
- Exception: If termination violates Standing Orders (e.g., no notice given) OR
  discriminatory (terminated for pregnancy, caste, gender - violation of
  Constitution Article 15-16)
```

---

## Performance Evaluation Process

### 1. Evaluation Frequency

**Best Practice Timeline**:

```
MONTH 1: Induction & Onboarding
- Day 1-7: HR induction (policies, benefits, IT setup)
- Day 8-30: Department induction (team introductions, product training, role shadowing)
- Evaluation: Informal check-in with manager (settle in, questions answered)

MONTH 2-3: Initial Performance Assessment
- KPIs set: Specific, measurable goals for probation period
- Mid-probation review (Month 3):
  - Manager evaluation (performance vs KPIs, behavioral competencies)
  - 360-degree feedback (peers, cross-functional teams)
  - Self-assessment (employee's view on progress, challenges)
- Outcome:
  - On track: Continue probation, no intervention
  - Needs improvement: Performance Improvement Plan (PIP) for remaining 3 months
  - Unsatisfactory: Consider termination (with 30 days notice)

MONTH 4-5: Performance Improvement (if PIP issued)
- Weekly check-ins with manager (progress tracking)
- Additional training/mentoring provided
- Month 5 review: Assess improvement

MONTH 6: Final Evaluation & Confirmation Decision
- Comprehensive evaluation:
  - Performance vs KPIs (quantitative metrics)
  - Behavioral competencies (teamwork, communication, initiative)
  - Cultural fit (alignment with company values)
  - Potential for growth (can take on increased responsibilities)
- Decision:
  - Option A: CONFIRM (performance satisfactory, issue confirmation letter)
  - Option B: EXTEND probation (borderline performance, extend to Month 9/12)
  - Option C: TERMINATE (performance unsatisfactory, 30 days notice)
```

### 2. Evaluation Criteria

**Quantitative Metrics (Role-Specific)**:

**Software Engineer**:
- Code quality: <5 bugs per 1,000 lines of code (industry standard)
- Sprint velocity: Complete assigned story points (target: 20 points per 2-week sprint)
- Code review feedback: Avg rating ≥3.5/5 from peer reviewers
- On-time delivery: ≥80% of tasks delivered by committed deadline

**Sales Executive**:
- Revenue: ₹30 lakh sales in 6 months (₹5 lakh/month avg)
- Lead conversion: 20% conversion rate (20 deals closed out of 100 leads)
- Customer acquisition: 15 new customers onboarded
- CRM hygiene: 100% leads updated in CRM within 24 hours

**HR Executive**:
- Recruitment: 12 positions filled in 6 months (2 hires/month avg)
- Time to hire: <45 days (from requisition to offer acceptance)
- Offer acceptance rate: ≥70% (7 out of 10 candidates accept offer)
- Employee engagement: Conduct 2 engagement activities (team outings, wellness sessions)

**Qualitative Metrics (Behavioral Competencies)**:

All Roles:
- **Teamwork**: Collaborates effectively, shares knowledge, supports colleagues
- **Communication**: Clear written/verbal communication, active listening
- **Initiative**: Proactively identifies problems, suggests solutions
- **Learning Agility**: Quickly learns new skills, adapts to feedback
- **Cultural Fit**: Aligns with company values (integrity, customer-focus, innovation)

**Evaluation Matrix** (Sample):

```
PROBATION EVALUATION FORM

Employee Name: Priya Sharma
Designation: Software Engineer
Department: Product Engineering
Probation Period: 1st Jan 2025 to 30th Jun 2025
Evaluation Date: 30th Jun 2025 (Month 6 Review)

A. QUANTITATIVE METRICS (Weight 60%):

1. Code Quality (20%)
   - Bugs per 1,000 lines: 4 bugs (Target: <5) → Rating: 4/5
   - Peer code review rating: 4.2/5 (Target: ≥3.5) → Rating: 5/5
   - Weighted Score: (4+5)/2 = 4.5 × 20% = 0.9

2. Sprint Velocity (20%)
   - Story points delivered: 18 avg (Target: 20) → Rating: 3.5/5
   - On-time delivery: 75% (Target: ≥80%) → Rating: 3/5
   - Weighted Score: (3.5+3)/2 = 3.25 × 20% = 0.65

3. Productivity (20%)
   - Tasks completed: 48 tasks in 6 months (Target: 50) → Rating: 4/5
   - Rework rate: 10% (Target: <15%) → Rating: 4/5
   - Weighted Score: (4+4)/2 = 4 × 20% = 0.8

QUANTITATIVE SCORE: (0.9 + 0.65 + 0.8) = 2.35/3.0 (78%)

B. QUALITATIVE METRICS (Weight 40%):

1. Teamwork (10%): Actively participates in team meetings, helps junior developers
   with code reviews → Rating: 4/5 → Score: 4 × 10% = 0.4

2. Communication (10%): Clear documentation, responsive on Slack, good stakeholder
   updates → Rating: 4.5/5 → Score: 4.5 × 10% = 0.45

3. Initiative (10%): Identified 3 process improvements (automated testing scripts,
   refactored legacy code) → Rating: 5/5 → Score: 5 × 10% = 0.5

4. Learning Agility (10%): Completed 2 certifications (AWS, React Advanced),
   quickly adapted to new tech stack → Rating: 5/5 → Score: 5 × 10% = 0.5

QUALITATIVE SCORE: (0.4 + 0.45 + 0.5 + 0.5) = 1.85/2.0 (92.5%)

C. OVERALL SCORE:
   Quantitative (60%): 2.35/3.0 = 78%
   Qualitative (40%): 1.85/2.0 = 92.5%
   TOTAL: (2.35 + 1.85) / 5.0 = 4.2/5.0 = 84%

D. RATING SCALE:
   - 4.5-5.0 (90-100%): Exceptional → CONFIRM + 10% salary hike
   - 4.0-4.49 (80-89%): Exceeds Expectations → CONFIRM + 5% salary hike
   - 3.5-3.99 (70-79%): Meets Expectations → CONFIRM
   - 3.0-3.49 (60-69%): Below Expectations → EXTEND probation by 3 months
   - <3.0 (<60%): Unsatisfactory → TERMINATE

E. DECISION: CONFIRM
   Employee has demonstrated solid performance (84% score), strong qualitative
   skills (initiative, learning agility), and good cultural fit. Sprint velocity
   slightly below target (18 vs 20), but improving trend observed in Month 5-6
   (20 points achieved in last sprint).

   RECOMMENDATION: Confirm as permanent employee with 5% salary hike (₹12 LPA
   to ₹12.6 LPA effective 1st July 2025).

Manager Signature: _______________
HR Signature: _______________
Date: 30th June 2025
```

---

## Probation Extension

### 1. Legal Requirements for Extension

**For Workmen** (Industrial Employment Standing Orders Act):
- **Original Probation**: 6 months
- **Extension**: Up to 6 additional months (12 months TOTAL maximum)
- **Requirements**:
  1. **Written Consent**: Employee must consent in writing to extension
  2. **Justification**: Clear performance gaps documented (not arbitrary)
  3. **Communicated Before Original Expiry**: Extension notice BEFORE Month 6 ends (if communicated after Month 6, extension invalid → employee auto-confirmed)

**For Managers/Supervisors** (Not Covered by Standing Orders):
- **Employer Discretion**: No statutory limit (common to extend 12 months to 18 months for senior roles)
- **Contractual Terms Govern**: Employment agreement specifies extension rights

**Standard Extension Clause** (Employment Agreement):
```
"The probation period may be extended by a further [3/6] months at the Company's
discretion if your performance is deemed unsatisfactory. Such extension will be
communicated in writing at least 7 days before the end of the original probation
period. Extension beyond [12] months total requires your written consent."
```

### 2. Extension Process

**Timeline**:
```
MONTH 5 (Week 3-4): Mid-Probation Review
- Manager identifies performance gaps (sprint velocity 15 vs target 20)
- Informal discussion with employee (areas for improvement)
- Decision: Consider extension if performance doesn't improve by Month 6

MONTH 6 (Week 1-2): Final Evaluation
- Performance still below target (18 vs 20 story points)
- Manager recommends extension (needs more time to assess improvement)
- HR drafts extension letter

MONTH 6 (Week 3, Day 15): Extension Letter Issued
- Timing: At least 7-14 days BEFORE original probation ends (30th June)
- Employee signs acknowledgment (written consent)

1st JULY: Extended Probation Begins
- Additional 3 months (till 30th September)
- Clear KPIs set for extended period (must achieve 20 story points/sprint consistently)
- Monthly reviews during extension (closer monitoring)

30th SEPTEMBER: Final Decision
- Performance improved: CONFIRM
- Performance still below: TERMINATE (with 30 days notice)
```

**Extension Letter Format**:
```
[Company Letterhead]

Date: 15th June 2025

Mr. Rohan Verma
Software Engineer
Product Engineering Team

Dear Rohan,

Subject: Extension of Probation Period

This is with reference to your probation period which is scheduled to end on
30th June 2025 (6 months from your joining date of 1st January 2025).

Based on your performance evaluation conducted on 10th June 2025, we have observed
that while you have demonstrated satisfactory progress in code quality and teamwork,
your sprint velocity (average 18 story points per sprint) has been below the
expected target of 20 story points.

In light of this, the Company has decided to extend your probation period by an
additional 3 months (from 1st July 2025 to 30th September 2025) to allow you
more time to demonstrate consistent performance at the required level.

EXTENDED PROBATION TERMS:
- Duration: 3 months (1st July 2025 to 30th September 2025)
- Performance Goals:
  a) Achieve 20 story points per sprint consistently (in 6 consecutive sprints)
  b) Maintain code quality (≤5 bugs per 1,000 lines)
  c) Improve on-time delivery to ≥85%
- Evaluation: Monthly reviews (end of July, August, September)
- Final Confirmation Decision: 30th September 2025

During the extended probation:
- Your salary and benefits remain unchanged
- All statutory entitlements (PF, leave, ESIC) continue
- Notice period remains 30 days (by either party)

Please sign and return the duplicate copy of this letter as acknowledgment and
consent for the extension.

We believe this additional time will help you achieve the required performance
standards and look forward to your continued improvement.

Yours sincerely,

[Signature]
Priya Mehta
HR Manager

EMPLOYEE ACKNOWLEDGMENT:
I acknowledge receipt of this letter and consent to the extension of my probation
period from 1st July 2025 to 30th September 2025.

Employee Signature: _______________
Date: _______________
```

---

## Confirmation Process

### 1. Confirmation Letter

**Issued After**: Satisfactory completion of probation (6/12 months)

**Confirmation Letter Format**:
```
[Company Letterhead]

Date: 1st July 2025

Ms. Priya Sharma
Software Engineer
Product Engineering Team

Dear Priya,

Subject: Confirmation of Employment

We are pleased to inform you that you have successfully completed your probation
period of 6 months (1st January 2025 to 30th June 2025) and your performance
has been found to be satisfactory.

Effective 1st July 2025, you are confirmed as a permanent employee of XYZ
Technologies Pvt Ltd in the position of Software Engineer.

CONFIRMATION BENEFITS:
1. Salary Revision: Your annual CTC is revised from ₹12,00,000 to ₹12,60,000
   (5% increment effective 1st July 2025)

2. Notice Period: Your notice period is now 90 days (increased from 30 days
   during probation), as per Clause 9 of your Employment Agreement

3. Eligible for Annual Performance Bonus: You will be eligible for the annual
   performance bonus (target: 12% of fixed salary, i.e., ₹1,51,200) for the
   performance year FY 2025-26, payable in April 2026

4. Eligible for Promotions: You are now eligible for annual promotion cycles
   (next review: April 2026)

5. Stock Options (ESOPs): You will be granted 500 stock options (vesting 25%
   per year over 4 years, exercise price ₹500) in the next ESOP grant cycle
   (August 2025)

All other terms and conditions of your Employment Agreement dated 1st January
2025 remain unchanged.

We congratulate you on your confirmation and look forward to your continued
contributions to the Company.

Yours sincerely,

[Signature]
Amit Kumar
Engineering Manager

[Signature]
Priya Mehta
HR Manager

EMPLOYEE ACKNOWLEDGMENT:
I acknowledge receipt of this confirmation letter.

Employee Signature: _______________
Date: _______________
```

### 2. Changes Upon Confirmation

**Rights Enhanced**:
1. **Longer Notice Period**: 60-90 days (vs 14-30 days during probation)
2. **Severance Eligibility**: If terminated without cause, eligible for severance pay (typically 1-3 months salary, company policy)
3. **Greater Job Security**: Termination for cause requires domestic inquiry (Principles of Natural Justice)
4. **Bonus/ESOP Eligibility**: Performance bonus, stock options (if applicable)
5. **Promotion Eligibility**: Enters annual promotion cycle

**Obligations Remain**:
1. **Confidentiality**: Continues (perpetual for trade secrets)
2. **Non-Compete**: Enforceable post-confirmation (12-18 months post-exit)
3. **IP Assignment**: All IP created belongs to employer
4. **Notice Period**: Must serve or pay in lieu

---

## Termination During Probation

### 1. Employer-Initiated Termination

**Grounds**:
- Unsatisfactory performance (most common)
- Misconduct (theft, insubordination, absenteeism)
- Background verification failure (post-joining discovery of false information)
- Position no longer required (business reasons)

**Process**:
1. **Notice**: 14-30 days written notice OR payment in lieu (as per employment agreement)
2. **No Domestic Inquiry**: Not required for probation termination (unless Standing Orders mandates)
3. **Payment**: Salary for notice period (if not served) + leave encashment
4. **No Severance**: No retrenchment compensation under Section 25F Industrial Disputes Act

**Termination Letter Format**:
```
[Company Letterhead]

Date: 15th May 2025

Mr. Karan Malhotra
Junior Executive - Sales
Sales Department

Dear Karan,

Subject: Termination of Employment During Probation Period

This is with reference to your employment with the Company as Junior Executive -
Sales, which commenced on 1st January 2025 on a probationary basis for 6 months.

Based on your performance evaluation conducted during the probation period, we
regret to inform you that your performance has not met the expected standards
for the role. Specifically:

1. Sales Target: You achieved ₹15 lakh sales against a target of ₹30 lakh
   (50% achievement) during the 4.5-month period.

2. Lead Conversion: Your conversion rate was 8% (8 deals closed out of 100 leads)
   against the target of 20%.

3. Customer Feedback: Received negative feedback from 3 customers regarding
   follow-up and responsiveness.

Despite coaching and support provided by your manager, the required improvement
has not been observed.

Accordingly, the Company has decided to terminate your employment during the
probation period, effective 14th June 2025 (30 days from the date of this letter,
as per your Employment Agreement Clause 9).

LAST WORKING DAY: 14th June 2025

FULL & FINAL SETTLEMENT:
You will receive the following in your final settlement (payable by 30th June 2025):

1. Salary for notice period (15th May to 14th June): ₹25,000 (pro-rated for
   30 days)
2. Leave encashment: 7 days earned leave accrued (4.5 months × 1.5 days/month)
   encashed at Basic salary (₹14,000 × 7/26 = ₹3,769)
3. Reimbursements: Pending expense claims (if any, submit by 20th May)

DEDUCTIONS:
- Company laptop (HP ProBook) not returned: ₹45,000 (will be deducted from
  final settlement, please return by 14th June to avoid deduction)

You are requested to:
a) Hand over all company property (laptop, access card, documents)
b) Complete the exit formalities with HR (exit interview, asset return form)
c) Provide your updated bank account details for final settlement credit

We wish you the best in your future endeavors.

Yours sincerely,

[Signature]
Ramesh Rao
Sales Manager

[Signature]
HR Department
```

### 2. Employee-Initiated Resignation During Probation

**Process**:
- **Notice**: 14-30 days (as per employment agreement, shorter than post-confirmation)
- **Payment**: No buyback clause typically enforced during probation (vs post-confirmation where notice buyout common)

**Resignation Letter** (Employee):
```
[Date: 10th April 2025]

To,
The HR Manager
ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd
Bangalore

Subject: Resignation from Employment

Dear Sir/Madam,

I am writing to formally resign from my position as Software Engineer with ABC
Technologies Pvt Ltd, effective 10th May 2025 (30 days from today, as per my
Employment Agreement).

I have decided to pursue a different career opportunity that aligns better with
my long-term goals.

I will ensure a smooth handover of my responsibilities and complete all pending
tasks during the notice period. Please let me know the exit formalities I need
to complete.

Thank you for the opportunity to work with ABC Technologies.

Yours sincerely,

[Signature]
Priya Sharma
Employee ID: ABC12345
```

---

## Common Issues & Resolutions

### Issue 1: Automatic Confirmation After Probation?

**Misconception**: "If employer doesn't issue extension/termination letter by end of Month 6, employee is auto-confirmed"

**Reality**:
- **Standing Orders Establishments**: YES, if employer doesn't communicate extension/termination before probation ends, employee deemed confirmed (per Model Standing Orders Rule 2)
- **Non-Standing Orders Establishments**: Depends on employment agreement
  - If agreement states "confirmation subject to satisfactory performance" → Not automatic
  - If agreement silent → Courts may interpret as automatic confirmation after probation period

**Best Practice**: Employer should issue confirmation/extension/termination letter AT LEAST 7-14 days before probation ends (avoid disputes)

### Issue 2: Probation Extension Without Written Consent

**Scenario**: Employer verbally tells employee "Your probation is extended by 3 months" but no written extension letter issued

**Legal Position**:
- **Workmen**: Extension invalid (Standing Orders require written communication + employee consent)
- **Result**: Employee deemed confirmed from original probation end date (6 months)
- **Employer Cannot Terminate**: Must now give 60-90 days notice (post-confirmation notice period) + may need domestic inquiry if terminating for cause

**Landmark Case**: Management of RSRTC v. Shyam Sunder (SC 2001)
- **Facts**: Shyam Sunder's probation was to end on 31st March. On 5th April (AFTER probation ended), employer issued extension letter (extending probation by 3 months)
- **Held**: Extension invalid (communicated after original probation expired). Shyam Sunder deemed confirmed from 1st April. Employer cannot now treat him as probationer.
- **Principle**: Extension must be communicated BEFORE original probation ends, else employee auto-confirmed

---

## Compliance Checklist

### For Employers

- [ ] Probation period specified in offer letter + employment agreement (6-12 months depending on role)
- [ ] Performance evaluation criteria defined upfront (KPIs, behavioral competencies)
- [ ] Evaluation frequency scheduled (Month 3, Month 6 reviews mandatory)
- [ ] Extension letter issued 7-14 days BEFORE probation ends (if extending)
- [ ] Written consent obtained from employee for extension (for workmen covered by Standing Orders)
- [ ] Confirmation letter issued promptly after probation completion (within 7 days of end date)
- [ ] Salary revision (if any) effective from confirmation date
- [ ] Notice period updated in HR systems (14-30 days probation → 60-90 days post-confirmation)
- [ ] Statutory benefits continued during probation (PF, ESIC, leave accrual)

### For Employees

- [ ] Understand probation duration (6/12 months) and evaluation criteria
- [ ] Review performance goals set at Month 1 (ensure specific, measurable, achievable)
- [ ] Prepare for mid-probation review (Month 3, document achievements)
- [ ] Seek feedback proactively (don't wait for formal reviews, monthly check-ins with manager)
- [ ] If extension letter received, review carefully (reasons for extension, goals for extended period)
- [ ] Provide written consent for extension (if required)
- [ ] If terminated during probation, check notice period compliance (employer must give 14-30 days or pay in lieu)
- [ ] Claim full & final settlement (salary + leave encashment, typically paid within 30-45 days of last working day)

---

3. **IL-LABOUR-TERMINATION-FOR-CAUSE.md**: Termination during probation (simpler process) vs post-confirmation termination (domestic inquiry required)
